### Israel Fund — money-in source switched from `gifts_cache` to `israel_fund_raised_cache` (2026-06-08)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00619-zsl`. The Israel Fund page now reads Money In from a dedicated, durable cache table instead of running a live ILIKE over `gifts_cache`. The previous architecture missed any Israel Fund donation older than Veracross's `/v3/development/gifts` API window (~ recent year). Seeded from Veracross query 1077990 (June 8 2026 export) — 43 initiatives, $1,841,649.79 total.

**New table — `israel_fund_raised_cache`**:

```
id            uuid PK
workspace_id  uuid FK → workspaces(id)
event_name    text NOT NULL
raised        numeric DEFAULT 0
gift_count    integer DEFAULT 0
seed_raised   numeric DEFAULT 0
last_updated  timestamptz DEFAULT now()
UNIQUE (workspace_id, event_name)
```

Indexed on `(workspace_id, event_name)`. RLS uses the standard `workspace_isolation` policy.

**`seed_raised` / `raised` pattern**. `seed_raised` is the frozen baseline from the CSV-style INSERT used to seed the table — it's set once and never decreased. `raised` is the displayed total: `MAX(live total, seed_raised)`. This guarantees the historical baseline survives even when Veracross rolls older gifts off its API window. New events (no row yet) are inserted with `seed_raised = 0` — they only exist because Veracross reported them now, so there's no baseline to protect.

**New library — `lib/israelFundNormalization.ts`**. One exported function:

```ts
export function normalizeIsraelFundEvent(rawName: string): string | null
```

Strips `APL:` / `DEV:` prefixes, then applies named rules:
- `Columbus Baseball Tournament` → `null` (dropped entirely; passes the External Funds filter but doesn't belong on the page).
- Anything starting with `Kfar Aza` → `Kfar Aza` (collapses all sub-events).
- `Latet - Purim 24` or any `/Latet Israel Pesach/i` → `Latet`.
- Anything matching `/resilience/i` or `/baking for a better world/i` → `Israel Crisis`.
- `Yeshivat Hesder Kiryat Shmona Israel` → `Kiryat Shemona Yeshivah`.
- `Israel - Omri Hadad` → `Education Sayeret Matkal Soldier`.
- Everything else: prefix-stripped name passed through.

New normalization rules belong in this one file — the route reads from the cache, the sync writes to the cache, and both agree on the canonical name set.

**New library — `lib/syncIsraelFundRaised.ts`**. Exported function:

```ts
export async function syncIsraelFundRaisedCache(
  workspaceId: string,
): Promise<{ success: boolean; updated: number; inserted: number; error?: string }>
```

Pipeline:
1. Paginate `gifts_cache` for all rows with `fundraising_activity ILIKE '%External Funds%' AND event IS NOT NULL` (no gift_type filter — spec).
2. Group by `normalizeIsraelFundEvent(event)`, summing amounts. Events that normalize to `null` are dropped.
3. Read existing `seed_raised` values for the workspace in one query.
4. Upsert payload built per the spec's `WHERE EXCLUDED.raised > seed_raised` rule:
   - **New events** (no existing row): insert with `seed_raised = 0`, `raised = live total`, `gift_count = live count`.
   - **Existing events with live total > seed**: update `raised = live`, keep `seed_raised`, update `gift_count` and `last_updated`.
   - **Existing events with live total ≤ seed**: skipped entirely. The row keeps its seeded values — `gift_count` and `last_updated` don't get stomped when Veracross drops older gifts off the API window.
   - **Events with no live data at all**: never visited, so their seeded rows stay intact.
5. Logs `inserted` / `updated` counts.

**Hook into `lib/syncGifts.ts`**. `syncGiftsForWorkspace` calls `syncIsraelFundRaisedCache(workspaceId)` after `gifts_sync_meta` is written. Independent failure mode — the raised_cache sync throwing or returning `!success` does NOT mark the gifts sync as failed; the gifts upsert is the primary contract. Errors are warned to logs. This means the Cloud Functions `syncGiftsHourlyWeekdays` (`0 * * * 1-5`) and `syncGiftsDailyWeekends` (`0 6 * * 0,6`) automatically refresh the Israel Fund raised_cache too, without any change to `functions/src/index.ts`.

**Route refactor — `app/api/development/israel-fund/route.ts`**. Money-In logic entirely replaced with a single `SELECT event_name, raised, gift_count FROM israel_fund_raised_cache WHERE workspace_id = ? ORDER BY raised DESC`. The `fetchIsraelGrantsPaidOut` Google Sheets helper, the `fetchMoneyIn` paginated `gifts_cache` reader, the `stripPrefixes` helper, the `parseSheetAmount` helper, and the `grantsKeySet` whitelist — all removed. Money-Out + grants table aggregation (`SUM funding_amount WHERE is_visible AND wire_was_sent AND NOT grant_not_given`) unchanged. Response shape unchanged so `IsraelFundTab.tsx` keeps rendering without UI changes: `total_raised`, `total_paid_out` (= `total_disbursed`, back-compat), `total_disbursed`, `total_balance`, `initiatives[]`, `grants[]`, `veracrossEvents[]` (now sourced from raised_cache event names), `moneyIn[]` (now `{ event, total, pledgeBalance: 0, giftCount }` — `pledgeBalance` is always 0 since the cache rolls up cash-received totals only).

**Authoritative going forward**. `israel_fund_raised_cache` is the source of truth for Israel Fund money-in. New Veracross events automatically appear on the page via the next gifts sync (hourly weekdays / 6am weekends ET) — no code change. To override or hand-edit a value, write directly to the table; the sync's "only update when live > seed" rule means hand-edited `raised` values stick around as long as no live total exceeds them. Set `seed_raised` to the same value if you want to make the override permanent.

**Untouched**: `IsraelFundTab.tsx` UI, Cooper Fund, Guardian Circle, Overview, Weekly Gifts, Capital Campaign, Campaigns, Admin, Buzz, all other routes. `buzzConversation.ts` still has its own `israelFundBalance` derivation reading from `gifts_cache` — that path was left alone per spec and is a candidate cleanup item to migrate to the new cache.

### Buzz — Phase 2.5 write-back: mark Monday + Command Center tasks done from Slack (2026-06-08)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00613-frd`. Buzz can now write back to Monday.com and the Command Center tasks table when a user asks ("mark X done"). Phase 2 was read-only; this lands the first action capability without rewriting the prompt loop into Claude `tool_use`.

**New library — `lib/mondayActions.ts`**:

```ts
export async function markMondayItemDone(
  boardId: string,
  itemId: string,
  statusColumnId: string,
  apiKey: string,
): Promise<{ success: boolean; error?: string }>
```

Issues a Monday `change_column_value` GraphQL mutation against `https://api.monday.com/v2` with `value: "{\"label\":\"Done\"}"`. If the response surfaces a label-related error (response carries an `errors` array containing the word `label` or `value`), retries once with `"Completed"` since boards differ on which exact label text is configured. Auth header matches the existing read path: `Authorization: <apiKey>` (no `Bearer` prefix). Never throws — wraps everything in try/catch and always returns the result object so the calling handler can append a friendly Slack line.

**`lib/morningBriefing.ts` — `fetchMondayTasks` now captures `statusColumnId`**. The GraphQL query already selected `column_values { id text type }`. The mapping loop now identifies the status column by either `column.id.includes('status')` or `column.type === 'color'` (legacy) or `column.type === 'status'` (modern), records the column id on the parsed `MondayTask`, and uses its text as the status (falling back to `colById['status']` for boards that explicitly use that id). The `MondayTask.statusColumnId?: string` field was previously declared but unset; it's populated now.

**`lib/buzzConversation.ts` — marker-based write-back**:

- `TaskRow` gained `id`; `fetchAllUserTasks` selects it. `MondayTask` gained `statusColumnId`; `fetchMondayTasksIfSet` populates it using the same column-detection logic as the briefing path. `ContextBundle` gained `mondayBoardId` (carried through from `user_briefing_preferences.monday_board_id`).
- System prompt rendering exposes `(task_id: <uuid>)` after each CC task line and `(item_id: <id>, status_column_id: <id>)` after each Monday task line. The rules block has a "Never speak ids back to the user" instruction plus the **MONDAY WRITE-BACK** and **COMMAND CENTER TASK WRITE-BACK** sections that tell Claude to emit a marker at the very end of its reply.
- Marker shapes (both end-of-reply, parsed with `/<tag>([\s\S]*?)<\/tag>/`):
  ```
  <monday_action>{"action":"mark_done","item_id":"...","item_name":"...","status_column_id":"..."}</monday_action>
  <cc_action>{"action":"mark_done","task_id":"<uuid>","task_name":"..."}</cc_action>
  ```
- `applyWriteBacks(reply, ctx)` runs after `callClaude`. It parses each marker, strips it from the user-visible reply, executes the corresponding action (`markMondayItemDone` for Monday, direct `supabaseAdmin.from('tasks').update({ status: 'done', completed_at, updated_at })` for CC tasks — internal service pattern, not an HTTP PATCH to `/api/tasks`, since the Slack webhook has no user session), and appends either `\n✅ Marked *NAME* as done…` or `\n⚠️ Couldn't update…` to the reply. Stripped reply (without markers) is persisted to `buzz_conversation_history` so subsequent turns don't re-trigger.
- Defense in depth: the marked item is removed from `ctx.mondayTasks` / `ctx.myTasks` after success so any future logic in the same call doesn't see it. Real freshness comes from the next turn's full re-fetch (Monday items with `state === 'done'` are filtered out at read; CC tasks with `status === 'done'` are excluded by the existing `.not('status', 'in', '(done,archived)')` filter).

**Claim disambiguation**. The system prompt tells Claude: "If the user names something that could match either a Monday item or a CC task, ask which they mean." Claude is also told to ask for clarification when it can't identify the exact item, rather than guessing.

**Failure modes are non-fatal**. Monday API down → confirmation line becomes the ⚠️ fallback. Supabase update fails → same. Malformed JSON in the marker → the marker is silently dropped (parseMarker returns `null`) and no action runs. Markers without a matching action key are no-ops.

**Untouched**: morning briefing schedule, onboarding flow, `lib/buzzBot.ts`, all non-Buzz files. The `MondayTask` type lives in two places (briefing + conversation) by spec design — keeping the briefing path stable.

### Buzz — Phase 2 conversational Q&A live (2026-06-02)

The "coming soon" placeholder in `app/api/slack/events/route.ts` is gone. Buzz now replies to any DM with a real Claude-powered answer scoped to the user's permissions.

**New schema** — `buzz_conversation_history`:
- `(id uuid PK, workspace_id uuid FK→workspaces, email text, messages jsonb DEFAULT '[]', last_message_at, updated_at, UNIQUE(workspace_id, email))`.
- `messages` is an array of `{role: 'user'|'assistant', content: string, timestamp: ISO}` entries, trimmed to the last 10 turns on each save.
- Indexed on `(workspace_id, email)`. RLS uses the standard `workspace_isolation` policy.

**New library — `lib/buzzConversation.ts`**. Exports one function:

```ts
export async function handleConversationalMessage(
  member: ConversationMember,   // id, email, display_name, role, title, slack_user_id, allowed_modules
  messageText: string,
  workspaceId: string,
  anthropicApiKey: string,
  slackBotToken: string,
  dryRun: boolean,
): Promise<void>
```

Pipeline:
1. **Special-command short-circuit (before Claude is called):**
   - `'reset'` → deletes the user's `buzz_conversation_history` row, DMs "Conversation cleared. Fresh start! 🐝".
   - `'briefing'` → calls `generateAllBriefings({ dryRun: false, forceWeekend: true, singleUserEmail: member.email })`. The new `singleUserEmail` opt narrows the DB member query AND bypasses the `BUZZ_TEST_MODE` allowlist so a user can always pull their own briefing on demand.
2. **Context bundle** — builds a permission-gated snapshot:
   - Always: `name`, `role`, `title`, `todayDate`, `myTasks` (open tasks via `assigned_to` candidates, top 20), `calendarThisWeek` (next 5 days via per-user Google OAuth).
   - If a Monday board ID is set on the pref row: `mondayTasks` (top 10 from that board, overdue-first sort).
   - If `role ∈ {owner, assistant}`: `studentAbsencesToday` from `attendance_cache` (`attendance_category = 1` for today, ET).
   - If `role = assistant`: `draftsAwaitingReview` (emails with `draft_status = 'draft_ready'`) and `grantsPendingWire` (visible Israel Fund grants with `wire_was_sent = false`).
   - If user has `development` module: `weeklyGifts` (Operating 2025-2026, gift_types 1/3, since week-start Monday), `israelFundBalance` (External Funds raised − wire-sent disbursed), `cooperFundBalance` (latest `cooper_fund_categories.current_balance` snapshot).
   - `preferencesSummary` from `user_briefing_preferences.preferences_summary`.
   - Modules the user can't see are simply absent; the system prompt instructs Claude to say "I don't have access to that data" when asked about something outside the bundle.
3. **History load** — last 10 entries from `buzz_conversation_history` for `(workspace_id, email)`, mapped to Claude's `{role, content}` shape (timestamp dropped on the way in).
4. **Claude call** — model `claude-sonnet-4-20250514`, `max_tokens: 400`. System prompt contains the rendered context bundle + the no-fabrication rules + "Use Slack markdown".
5. **Slack DM** — if `dryRun === false` and a bot token is present, POST to `chat.postMessage`. Under `DRY_RUN = true`, the prompt + reply are logged with `[BUZZ CONVO - DRY RUN]`.
6. **History save** — upsert (existence-check + update or insert, no ON CONFLICT) with the new user/assistant pair appended; final array trimmed to 10. History is persisted even under DRY_RUN so previewing builds realistic transcripts.

**Approach note** — Phase 2 uses a **static context snapshot**, not Claude `tool_use`. Every reply gets the same bundle shape regardless of question. This keeps Phase 2 simple and predictable. **Phase 2.5 (planned)** swaps the snapshot for tool definitions so Claude can pull on-demand data — arbitrary date ranges, named-donor lookups, calendar searches past the 5-day window, etc. — without bloating every prompt.

**Wire-in — `app/api/slack/events/route.ts`**:
- The member query now selects `role, title, allowed_modules` in addition to the previous fields.
- The onboarding-complete branch's old `PHASE_2_PLACEHOLDER_MESSAGE` send is replaced with a direct `handleConversationalMessage(...)` call using the already-loaded `botToken` and `anthropicKey`.
- `PHASE_2_PLACEHOLDER_MESSAGE` is no longer imported (still exported from `lib/buzzBot.ts` for now — harmless dead export, can be removed in a cleanup pass).

**`lib/morningBriefing.ts`** picked up one signature change to support the `'briefing'` command:
- `generateAllBriefings(opts)` now accepts an optional `singleUserEmail`. When set, the `workspace_members` query is narrowed to `.eq('email', singleUserEmail)` AND the `BUZZ_TEST_MODE` allowlist filter is bypassed (`(BUZZ_TEST_MODE && !opts.singleUserEmail) ? filter : all`). All other behavior is unchanged.

### Israel Fund: grant CRUD UI live — `isEmily` viewer-vs-editor split (2026-06-03)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00594-lk8`.

Full UI half of the grants system shipped to `IsraelFundTab.tsx`. Backend (table + CRUD + seed from the earlier 2026-06-03 deploy) was already deployed; this rewrite was UI-only — no API routes touched.

**`isEmily` detection pattern**. Component reads `useAuth().user.email` from `AuthProvider`, lowercases it, and compares against the `EMILY_EMAIL = 'egray@saracademy.org'` module constant — matches the API's `EMILY_EMAIL` in `app/api/development/israel-fund/grants/[id]/route.ts`. The boolean drives every editor-only branch (add/edit/delete buttons, eye toggle, the additional sub-table columns). Non-Emily viewers never see hidden grants at all; Emily sees them muted with a closed-eye icon. Pattern is intentionally local to this tab — there's no app-wide "editor email" concept yet; clone the same constant + `useAuth` read if another tab grows similar Emily-only affordances.

**Initiative summary table now expandable**. Replaced the prior inline `DonorAnnotations` expansion with a per-initiative grants sub-table. Each summary row gets a chevron (right → down on expand) + name + Raised + Disbursed + Balance (pill, green ≥ 0 / red < 0). Emily-only "+ Add" button at the far right of each row opens the panel pre-filled with that initiative. Hidden + grant-not-given rows are still excluded from the disbursed total (backend handles that aggregation); initiatives with zero raised + zero visible-paid disappear (client-side filter mirrors the backend rule).

**Sub-table column visibility split**. Everyone sees: Grant # | Organization/Person | What they're funding | Amount | Date Wire Sent | Notes. Emily additionally sees: Category | Confirmed Payment | Submitted By | Contact Info | Wire Status | Procurify | Not Given | Wire Sent | Visible (eye toggle) | Edit (pencil) | Delete (trash). Grant Not Given rows show a red "Not Given" badge inline next to the grant number for everyone.

**Add/Edit panel** — 480px slide-in from the right, matches the Guardian Circle donor sidebar pattern (`fixed top-0 right-0 bottom-0 w-full max-w-[480px]` + `bg-slate-900/20 z-40` backdrop). Triggered by either "+ Add" on a summary row (pre-fills initiative + suggested next grant number) or the edit pencil on a sub-table row (pre-fills all fields). The Initiative field is an `<input list="…">` + `<datalist>` combobox sourced from the union of `veracrossEvents[]` (route returns this for the picker) and any unique initiative names already on a grant row. Form maps directly to the API payload via `formToPayload`; `funding_amount` is parsed from a string input on submit. Save routes to POST (new) or PATCH (edit). On success, the local `grants[]` state is updated and `deriveInitiatives()` re-derives summary rows client-side so totals update without a refetch. `raisedByKey` is a frozen baseline taken from the initial response — Veracross numbers never change client-side.

**Optimistic toggles + safety**. Eye toggle does an immediate local state flip then PATCH; reverts + toasts on error. Delete prompts with `window.confirm("Delete Grant [#]? This cannot be undone.")`, then DELETE, then toast.

**Chart**. Recharts horizontal `BarChart` (`layout="vertical"`) below the initiatives table — top 12 by raised, dual bars per initiative (`#3b82f6` blue raised, `#f87171` rose disbursed). Y-axis labels truncated to 20 chars + ellipsis (tooltip shows the full name via `labelFormatter` reading `payload[0].payload.name`); X-axis uses an `axisMoney(v)` formatter that emits `$Xk` / `$XM`. 420px container height. Section title: "Raised vs. Disbursed by Initiative".

**Footnote rewrite**: "Raised: Veracross gifts data · Disbursed: Israel Fund Grants (managed by Emily Gray) · Updated live". The legacy footnote that referenced the Google Sheets tracker is gone.

**Back-compat dropped UI-side**. `IsraelFundTab.tsx` no longer reads `total_paid_out` / `paidOut` — `total_disbursed` + `disbursed` are used everywhere. The route still returns the aliases for safety; removing them route-side is the outstanding TODO. `DonorAnnotations` is no longer rendered on this tab; the underlying `donor_notes` / `donor_tags` rows tagged `Israel: <initiative>` aren't deleted, just hidden — if they need to be surfaced again, restore the dynamic import + the `tagsByInitiative` bulk fetch.

**Untouched**: any API route (the backend was already deployed), Cooper Fund, Guardian Circle, Weekly Gifts, Campaigns, Capital Campaign, Overview, admin pages, auth.

### Buzz — Monday.com per-user board pulldown in morning briefings (2026-06-05)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00610-kn7`. Each user can optionally point Buzz at one Monday board; Buzz pulls the top open items into their morning briefing.

**Schema**: `user_briefing_preferences` gained `monday_board_id text` (migration `add_monday_board_id_to_briefing_prefs`). Becca's row pre-set to `'4230192042'`. To enable for another user, write the board ID into their row via Admin → Onboarding (UI uses the existing GET/PATCH endpoint — board ID column to be added in a follow-up admin edit) or run a one-off `UPDATE user_briefing_preferences SET monday_board_id = '...' WHERE email = '...'`.

**Briefing-side logic** (`lib/morningBriefing.ts`):
- The pref-row select gained `monday_board_id`.
- After `fetchModuleData(...)`, if `prefRow.monday_board_id` is set and non-blank, the loop calls `fetchMondayTasks(boardId, todayIso)` and assigns the result to `modules.mondayTasks`. If the column is null/empty for that user, the Monday call is skipped entirely.
- `fetchMondayTasks()` POSTs to `https://api.monday.com/v2` using `MONDAY_API_KEY` from env. GraphQL query: `boards(ids:[$id]) { items_page(limit:20) { items { id name state column_values { id text } } } }`. Items with `state` in `('done','archived','deleted')` are filtered out. From `column_values` it extracts:
  - Due date: `date4` or `due_date` (whichever is set first), validated as `YYYY-MM-DD` prefix.
  - Status: `status` text value.
  - Priority: `priority` text value.
- Sort: overdue (due < today) first, then due_date asc with nulls last, then by priority text (`high`/`critical`/`urgent` → `med` → `low` → other). Top 5 returned.
- **Failure modes are always quiet returns** (`[]`): missing `MONDAY_API_KEY`, non-2xx response, GraphQL `errors` array, parse exception. Monday outages never break the rest of the briefing.

**Prompt-side logic** (`buildClaudePrompt`):
- When `modules.mondayTasks` has ≥1 item, a Monday section is inserted between Tasks and the other module lines:
  ```
  Monday.com to-do board (top items):
  • Item name (due 2026-06-10) [High]
  • …
  (Per user preference: focus on the top 3 most timely and important)
  ```
- When empty, the block is omitted entirely (no header pollution).
- `moduleDataSummary` in the briefing result gained `mondayTasksCount` so the admin preview cards can show the fetched count alongside calendar/task counts.

**No onboarding-question change** — `monday_board_id` is admin-set (per Becca's spec note), not user-volunteered, to keep onboarding short. Users with no board ID get the existing briefing shape unchanged.

### Buzz — test-mode allowlist + weekend skip + richer onboarding (2026-06-05)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00604-48l`. Four targeted hardening fixes layered on top of the Phase 1 + Phase 2 build.

- **`BUZZ_TEST_MODE` allowlist** (`lib/buzzBot.ts`): `BUZZ_TEST_MODE: boolean` + `BUZZ_TEST_USERS: string[]` constants. While `BUZZ_TEST_MODE === true`, `generateAllBriefings()` post-filters the workspace member list down to `BUZZ_TEST_USERS` (currently Becca + Emily). Logs `[BUZZ] Test mode active — sending to: ...` at the top of every run so it's impossible to miss in Cloud Run logs. Flip to `false` to roll out to everyone with `slack_user_id` set.
- **Weekend skip** (`lib/morningBriefing.ts`): the entry point checks `new Date().getDay()` and returns `[]` on Sat (6) / Sun (0). New `opts.forceWeekend?: boolean` overrides — the **preview endpoint passes `forceWeekend: true`** so Becca can iterate on any day; the **internal endpoint and scheduled function do NOT pass it**, so the cron silently no-ops on weekends without any holiday calendar logic. Cron schedule unchanged at `'30 7 * * 1-5'` (weekdays only — schedule still commented out as the second safety layer).
- **Richer onboarding questions** (`lib/buzzBot.ts` `ONBOARDING_MESSAGE`): replaces the single-question version with a four-question intro covering (1) where the user keeps tasks, (2) whether Buzz should scan email, (3) Calendar source-of-truth, (4) what they most want to know first thing. Closes with "Reply here with whatever comes naturally — you don't need to answer in order. I'll figure it out." Claude still summarizes the free-text reply into `preferences_summary` via `summarizeOnboardingReply()`.
- **Stars removed from name**: `*Buzz*` → `Buzz` in both `ONBOARDING_MESSAGE` and `ONBOARDING_SAVED_MESSAGE`. Emoji 🐝 stays.

Approval path unchanged from the Phase 1 doc. To remove the test-mode gate after rollout: set `BUZZ_TEST_MODE = false` in `lib/buzzBot.ts` (one constant) and redeploy.

### Slack AI Assistant — Buzz Phase 1 + Phase 2 onboarding (2026-06-05)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00602-w8w`. Per-user morning briefing infrastructure for the Slack AI Assistant ("Buzz"). **`DRY_RUN = true` everywhere** — no Slack messages will be sent until Becca reviews preview output and flips the constant in `lib/morningBriefing.ts`. Scheduler is commented out in `functions/src/index.ts` as a second safety layer.

**Schema corrections discovered during build** (spec → actual):
- `workspace_members.sub_permissions text[]` doesn't exist — dropped from Randy's INSERT.
- `workspace_members.is_active boolean` doesn't exist — dropped from INSERT + briefing query.
- `workspace_members.user_id text NOT NULL` is required — Randy seeded with `'PLACEHOLDER_resulto'` per the session-route placeholder pattern (gets swapped to a real Firebase UID on first sign-in).
- `tasks.urgency` → `tasks.priority`; `tasks.assignee_key`/`owner_email` → `tasks.assigned_to`.
- `attendance_cache.attendance_category` is integer (1=absent), not string `'absent'`.
- `attendance_cache.student_id` doesn't exist — uses `person_id`.

**New table `user_briefing_preferences`** (migration `create_user_briefing_preferences`):
- Columns: `id` (uuid PK), `workspace_id` (FK), `email`, `onboarding_complete` (default false), `onboarding_sent_at`, `raw_preference`, `preferences_summary`, `preferred_time` (default '7:30'), `style_preference` (default 'concise'), `created_at`, `updated_at`. `UNIQUE(workspace_id, email)`.
- RLS workspace_isolation policy.

**Randy seeded** as `workspace_members` row: `resulto@saracademy.org` (placeholder email — verify with Becca; her spec noted the address is assumed), display "Randy Esulto", role `viewer`, slack_user_id `U03TBMX4HUK`, divisions `['academy','hs']`, title "Media Director".

**`lib/buzzBot.ts`** — bot constants: `BOT_NAME = 'Buzz'`, `BOT_EMOJI = '🐝'`, `ONBOARDING_MESSAGE(firstName)`, `ONBOARDING_SAVED_MESSAGE(firstName)`, `PHASE_2_PLACEHOLDER_MESSAGE`, `firstNameOf(displayName, email)`.

**`lib/morningBriefing.ts`** — core entry point `generateAllBriefings({ dryRun })`:
- Hard-coded `DRY_RUN = true` at the top — the only place to flip the kill switch.
- Loads Anthropic API key + Slack bot token via `getIntegration()` with env fallback.
- Loads workspace_members with `slack_user_id NOT NULL` for the SAR workspace.
- Per user: checks `user_briefing_preferences` first.
  - **Onboarding incomplete** → emits intro DM (logged in dry-run); upserts the pref row with `onboarding_sent_at = now()` so it doesn't spam on re-runs.
  - **Onboarding complete** → fetches Calendar (via per-user `getValidGoogleToken`), Tasks (`tasks.assigned_to IN [email, assignee_key, display_name]` + `due_date <= end-of-day-ET OR priority = 'urgent'`), Module data (attendance count for owners/assistants, drafts-awaiting-review + grants-pending-wire for assistants, weekly Operating gifts for development-module users). Builds Claude prompt with `preferences_summary` injected. Calls Sonnet 4 (`claude-sonnet-4-20250514`, 500 max tokens). Returns synthesized text.
- Result shape: `{ userId, name, email, slackUserId, kind: 'briefing'|'onboarding'|'skipped', message, moduleDataSummary, calendarEventCount, taskCount, preferencesUsed?, onboardingJustSent, error? }`.
- Also exports `summarizeOnboardingReply(apiKey, raw)` used by the Slack events webhook to summarize free-text replies before persisting.

**API routes**:
- `GET /api/slack/morning-briefing/preview` — admin-only (Becca's email or workspace owner); forces `dryRun=true` regardless of the constant. Returns `{ botName, dryRunConstant, previewForcedDryRun, generatedAt, briefings }`.
- `POST /api/slack/morning-briefing-internal` — shared-secret auth (`X-Internal-Secret`); honors `DRY_RUN` constant. Called by the Cloud Function. Excluded from middleware auth.
- `POST /api/slack/events` — Slack webhook for DMs. Verifies `X-Slack-Signature` (HMAC-SHA256 with replay-window check) **if** `signingSecret` is present in `workspace_integrations.slack.credentials` (degrades to unverified with a warning otherwise — initial rollout). Handles `url_verification` handshake. For `message.im` DMs from non-bots: looks up member by `slack_user_id`, returns 200 fast, processes async. If pref row missing or `onboarding_complete=false` → summarizes via Claude → upserts pref row with `onboarding_complete=true` → DMs ONBOARDING_SAVED_MESSAGE (logged in dry-run). Else → Phase 2 stub: logs the message and DMs `PHASE_2_PLACEHOLDER_MESSAGE`. Excluded from middleware auth.
- `GET/PATCH /api/slack/morning-briefing/onboarding` — admin-only management of `user_briefing_preferences` rows. GET joins display_name from workspace_members for table display. PATCH toggles `onboarding_complete` for the reset flow.

**Cloud Function** (`functions/src/index.ts`):
- `generateMorningBriefings` — HTTPS-triggered, POSTs to `/api/slack/morning-briefing-internal` with the shared secret. Safe to invoke now (DRY_RUN prevents Slack sends).
- `scheduledMorningBriefings` — **commented out**. To enable: confirm `DRY_RUN = false`, uncomment the block, redeploy `firebase deploy --only functions:scheduledMorningBriefings`. Schedule when enabled: `'30 7 * * 1-5'` (7:30am ET school days).

**Middleware** (`middleware.ts`): added `api/slack/events` and `api/slack/morning-briefing-internal` to the matcher exclusion list so the webhook + Cloud Function callback can POST without a Firebase session cookie.

**Admin UI** (`app/admin/permissions/page.tsx`): new 5th tab "Morning Briefings" with two sub-tabs:
- **Briefings** — DRY_RUN banner (amber when true, green when false). "Generate Preview" button hits the preview endpoint. Each result rendered as a card: name + Slack ID + email; kind pill (Briefing / Onboarding intro / Skipped); amber callout when onboarding is pending; formatted message in a `<pre>` block; footer with calendar event count, task count, and the preferences_summary used.
- **Onboarding** — table: Name / Onboarding Sent / Complete (green pill if true, amber Pending if false) / Preference Summary / Reset button. Reset PATCHes `onboarding_complete=false` so the user receives the intro DM again on next run. Lazy-loads on first open.

**Manual setup required (one-time, after approval)**:
1. **Slack app configuration** in `api.slack.com/apps`: Event Subscriptions → Enable → Request URL `https://ssrrbkcmdcenter-429508710310.us-east1.run.app/api/slack/events` → Subscribe to bot events `message.im` → Save + reinstall. **Until this is done, Buzz can send but not receive replies.**
2. **Upload Slack `signingSecret`** to `workspace_integrations.slack.credentials.signingSecret` (Admin → Integrations) so the events webhook rejects spoofed requests.
3. **Becca reviews preview output** at `/admin/permissions` → Morning Briefings → Generate Preview.
4. **Flip `DRY_RUN` to `false`** in `lib/morningBriefing.ts`.
5. **Uncomment `scheduledMorningBriefings`** in `functions/src/index.ts`.
6. **Redeploy** (Cloud Run + Functions).

### Israel Fund: `israel_fund_editor` sub-permission for grant CRUD (2026-06-03)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00598-4zw`. Replaces the hardcoded `isEmily` email check on the Israel Fund page with a proper sub-permission so Becca can grant edit rights to others without a code change.

**Pattern selected: Option A — register-and-enforce on the existing nested-JSONB sub-permission shape.** Verified before writing code: there is no `sub_permissions text[]` column anywhere; sub-permissions are stored as nested booleans inside `allowed_modules` JSONB (e.g. `{ "development": { "enabled": true, "israel_fund_editor": true } }`); `useWorkspace()` exposes `allowedModules`, not `subPermissions`; the canonical check is `hasSubPermission(allowedModules, moduleKey, subKey)` from `lib/modules.ts`; and **no other component in the codebase currently enforces a sub-permission** — Cooper Fund / offer_approvals are registry-only and dormant. This Israel Fund deploy is the first actual call site of `hasSubPermission()`. The pattern lights up for future features.

**Implementation**:
- `app/admin/permissions/page.tsx` — `SUB_PERMISSIONS.development` gained `{ key: 'israel_fund_editor', label: 'Israel Fund Editor', description: 'Can add, edit, and hide grant records on the Israel Fund page' }`. The existing Admin UI (Users tab → expand member → click Development chevron → check the box) now writes the value into `allowed_modules.development.israel_fund_editor` without any further code change.
- `app/components/development/IsraelFundTab.tsx`:
  - Imports `useWorkspace` (for `allowedModules` + `role`) and `hasSubPermission` (from `lib/modules.ts`).
  - Computes `canEditGrants` impersonation-aware:
    ```ts
    const canEditGrants =
      hasSubPermission(allowedModules, 'development', 'israel_fund_editor') ||
      effectiveEmail === EMILY_EMAIL ||
      effectiveEmail === BECCA_EMAIL ||
      role === 'owner';
    ```
  - All previous `isEmily` references swapped to `canEditGrants`.
- **No Supabase migration**, no session/auth changes.

**Why the email fallback stays**: Emily's and Becca's `workspace_members.allowed_modules` is `null` (the "no member restrictions = full module access" shortcut for owners/assistants, per `lib/modules.ts:11-12`). But `hasSubPermission()` returns `false` for null `allowedModules`, so a null row would lose edit access without the fallback. Workspace owners get edit rights by default via the `role === 'owner'` clause. To grant access to a fourth person, Becca opens `/admin/permissions`, expands their row's Development module sub-permissions, and ticks "Israel Fund Editor" — no code change.

**Pattern available for re-use**: any future page that wants the same shape — `hasSubPermission(allowedModules, 'someModule', 'someSubPerm') || hardcoded-email-fallback || role === 'owner'` — should follow this template until/unless we ever switch to the `sub_permissions text[]` model (deferred; not currently needed).

### Israel Fund: grants moved from Google Sheet → first-class Supabase table (2026-06-03)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00592-2zt`. The Israel Fund disbursement pipeline now reads from `israel_fund_grants` instead of Emily's "Master EG" Google Sheet. CRUD API + seed script let Emily manage grants in-app while keeping the same per-initiative join behavior.

**New Supabase table `israel_fund_grants`** (migration `create_israel_fund_grants`). Full schema documented under the Databases section above (one row per grant; RLS-isolated by workspace; service-role bypass for all app reads).

**New API routes**:
- `GET /api/development/israel-fund/grants` — paginated full list for the active workspace, ordered by `date_received DESC NULLS LAST, grant_number ASC`. Module-gated.
- `POST /api/development/israel-fund/grants` — insert. Requires `initiative` + numeric `funding_amount`. Whitelists which client-supplied fields make it into the row; `workspace_id` + timestamps are server-controlled.
- `PATCH /api/development/israel-fund/grants/[id]` — update any patchable field on the row. Validates that the target row belongs to the caller's workspace (404 / 403 otherwise). Overwrites `updated_at` on success.
- `DELETE /api/development/israel-fund/grants/[id]` — tightened ACL: only Emily Gray (`egray@saracademy.org`) or any workspace owner. Other users can still PATCH but can't remove rows.

**Main route refactor** (`app/api/development/israel-fund/route.ts`):
- Sheets fetch removed from the call site. `fetchIsraelGrantsPaidOut` (Google Sheets helper) is left in the file for rollback but is no longer invoked. `getGoogleAccessToken` import dropped.
- Disbursed totals now come from a paginated `israel_fund_grants` query with in-route aggregation: `SUM(funding_amount) WHERE is_visible AND wire_was_sent AND NOT grant_not_given GROUP BY initiative`.
- Response shape gained `grants: GrantRow[]` (full rows for per-initiative UI detail) and `veracrossEvents: string[]` (pre-whitelist event names for an initiative-picker dropdown). Old keys `total_paid_out` + `paidOut` are mirrored alongside the spec's new `total_disbursed` + `disbursed` so the current `IsraelFundTab.tsx` keeps rendering without changes — UI rename is a follow-up.

**Seed script** `scripts/seed-israel-fund-grants.ts` (npm `seed:israel-grants`). One-time import of Emily's CSV at `data/master-eg.csv`. Parses CSV (handles quoted commas + `""` escapes), maps 17 columns per the spec, skips header + blank rows, deletes any existing SAR-workspace rows, inserts in batches of 100. Date parser handles `M/D/YY` + `M/D/YYYY`; amount parser strips `$`/commas. Safe to re-run — non-idempotent re-inserts replaced by delete-then-insert per workspace.

**Operational steps for Becca**:
1. Place the CSV at `data/master-eg.csv`.
2. Run `npm run seed:israel-grants`.
3. Verify row count in Supabase (e.g. `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM israel_fund_grants WHERE workspace_id = 'a1b2c3d4-e5f6-7890-abcd-ef1234567890'`).
4. The Israel Fund page reads the new table automatically — no further code change needed.

**Untouched**: `IsraelFundTab.tsx` UI (paidOut / total_paid_out aliases preserved), Cooper Fund, Guardian Circle, Overview, Weekly Gifts, Campaigns, Capital Campaign, all other routes.

### Israel Fund: grants-sheet whitelist + raised gift-type fix + backfill param (2026-06-03)

Cloud Run revisions `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00586-hqz` (route fixes) and `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00590-2f7` (backfill iteration). Three independent fixes; the third surfaced a Veracross API limitation worth documenting.

**FIX 1 — Grants sheet is the dynamic whitelist** (`app/api/development/israel-fund/route.ts`). Previously the broadened `ILIKE %External Funds%` filter pulled unrelated Veracross events (Purim fundraisers, KCI Food Pantry, tour events) into the page. Now: after fetching Emily's grants tracker, build `grantsKeySet = new Set(grantsByInitiative.map(g => g.initiative.toLowerCase().trim()))`, then filter `moneyIn` to only events whose case-insensitive trimmed name is in that set. Initiatives that exist in the grants sheet but have no Veracross match still appear (`raised: 0, paidOut: g.paidOut`). The accordion's `moneyIn` payload uses the same `filteredMoneyIn` so the accordion + table stay in lockstep. Emily adds a new initiative to the sheet → it shows up on the next page load without a code change.

**FIX 2 — Raised total now uses cash-received gift types** (`israel-fund/route.ts`, both query paths). Switched `gift_type IN [1, 2]` → `gift_type IN [1, 3, 5]`. The Israel Fund tracks **cash actually received**:
- `1` = Donation (cash)
- `3` = payment against a pledge (cash received)
- `5` = soft credit (included per spec)
- `2` = pledge (uncashed) — **excluded**, since outstanding pledges aren't received cash

**Caveat for future readers**: this **deviates from the Cooper Fund route's gift-type model** (which treats 3 and 5 as soft-credit twins of 1/2 and excludes them to avoid double-counting). The interpretations differ by product intent + school config. If the Israel Fund "Total Raised" number on the page looks suspiciously inflated post-deploy (suggesting 3/5 really are double-counting their 1/2 parents), revisit. Inline comment in the route documents the assumption.

**FIX 3 — Backfill `start_date` param + Veracross API limitation discovered** (`app/api/development/backfill-gifts/route.ts`).
- Route now accepts `?start_date=YYYY-MM-DD` (validated against `^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$`); defaults to `years`-based start when unset (existing callers unaffected).
- **Server-side Veracross filter doesn't work.** First attempt: `?date_from=YYYY-MM-DD` → Veracross 400 "Unknown parameters". Second attempt: `?query_string=date>=...` → Veracross 400 `"Unknown parameters were provided: \"query_string\""`. Veracross's `/v3/development/gifts` exposes no server-side date filter.
- **Fallback**: client-side date filter at upsert time (`g.date >= startDateStr` lexicographic compare since Veracross dates are YYYY-MM-DD strings). Page cap bumped from 50 → 100. Errors now log the response body (first 500 chars) for fast iteration.
- **Production-blocker finding**: even with the full unfiltered paginated pull, **Veracross's `/v3/development/gifts` returns only ~recent gifts**. The 2026-06-03 backfill returned exactly 11,022 rows across all 100 pages — every row dated ≥ 2025-04-27. Post-backfill `gifts_cache` verification (Supabase query): `earliest_date = 2025-04-27`, `late_2023 = 0`, `in_2024 = 0`. No 2023 or 2024 history surfaced. Either Veracross has a built-in date floor on this endpoint, the school's API role doesn't include historical scope, or a different endpoint (`/v3/development/gifts/historical`?) holds the older data. The route is correctly implemented and the param works; the Israel Fund historical gap is a Veracross-side issue that needs a ticket. See master TODO.

**Manual backfill trigger** (documented for ops):
```bash
set -a && source .env.local && set +a
curl -sS -X POST "https://ssrrbkcmdcenter-429508710310.us-east1.run.app/api/development/backfill-gifts?start_date=2023-10-01" \
  -H "X-Internal-Secret: ${INTERNAL_SYNC_SECRET}" \
  -H "X-Workspace-Id: a1b2c3d4-e5f6-7890-abcd-ef1234567890" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json"
```
Verify completion via `gcloud run services logs read ssrrbkcmdcenter --region=us-east1 --project=rbk-cmd-center --limit=300 | grep BACKFILL`. The route returns 202 immediately; the actual paginated pull runs in the background via `next/server` `after()` and takes ~12 minutes for SAR's full gift volume. Both Firebase Hosting and Cloud Run gateways time out at 60s for the synchronous response but the background job continues.

The callout's headline total, the table rows, and the bottom-line "may be understated by $X" sentence all derive from the array, so adding/removing entries updates everything in one place. The whole callout is conditionally rendered on `FLAGGED_RECORDS.length > 0` — once Veracross fixes the API gap, emptying the array hides the callout entirely without a code change beyond the constant.

- Siegel (date 03/13/2025, $1,897)
- Levine (date 04/10/2025, $1,774)

Total surfaced: **$28,671**.

Constituent names link to `axiom.veracross.com/sar/#/detail/development-constituent/{id}/5011-general` when `constituentId` is non-null. The note column reads "Gift appears in Veracross reports but is excluded from /v3/development/gifts API response. Veracross ticket submitted 06/03/2026." ### Israel Fund: live Money Out from Emily's grants sheet (2026-06-03)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00582-48g`. Two-part refactor of `app/api/development/israel-fund/route.ts` + `IsraelFundTab.tsx`. The stale `israel_fund_summary` Supabase snapshot is no longer the source of truth for Money Out — paid-out totals now stream live from Emily's Israel Grants tracker Google Sheet.

**Route changes**:
- Money In filter widened from `fundraising_activity = 'External Funds General'` to `fundraising_activity ILIKE '%External Funds%'`. Catches newer variants alongside the historical bucket. Falls back to the exact-match filter if the broadened query throws or returns zero rows (defensive — both code paths exist in the route).
- New `fetchIsraelGrantsPaidOut(accessToken)` mirrors the Cooper Fund auth pattern verbatim: workspace-level token via `getGoogleAccessToken(effectiveWsId)`, sheet ID from `process.env.ISRAEL_GRANTS_SHEET_ID`, range `'Master EG'!A:Q`, returns `[]` on any failure. Sheet column mapping (0-indexed): D=INITIATIVE, L=FUNDING AMOUNT, Q=Wire Was Sent. Filters: wire === `"Yes"`, initiative not blank / `#N/A` / header `INITIATIVE`, amount > 0 after `$`/comma strip. Groups by INITIATIVE, sums amounts.
- Join logic: case-insensitive trim match between Veracross event names and grants-sheet initiative names. Veracross casing wins as the canonical display name; grants-only initiatives surface with `raised: 0` and `balance: -paidOut`.
- Response shape now: `{ total_raised, total_paid_out, total_balance, initiatives: [{ name, raised, paidOut, balance }], moneyIn, as_of }`. `israel_fund_summary` is no longer read — `pledges_not_paid` / `grants_paid` / `funds_not_claimed` / `updated_by` removed from the payload.

**UI changes** (`IsraelFundTab.tsx`):
- `Initiative` interface lost the `pledgesNotPaid` field; `IsraelData` updated to mirror the new shape.
- Three headline cards: **Total Raised** (green), **Total Paid Out** (red), **Balance at SAR** (green when ≥ 0, red when negative). Replaces the prior Grants Paid + Balance cards.
- Initiatives table unchanged in structure (Initiative / Raised / Paid Out / Balance) but renders the live joined data instead of the snapshot.
- New attribution footnote: "Raised: Veracross gifts data · Paid Out: Israel Grants tracker (Emily Gray) · Updated live".
- Date footer switched from snapshot `as_of_date` to the ISO `as_of` timestamp formatted as "MMM D, YYYY · h:mm a".

**Env / deploy**: `ISRAEL_GRANTS_SHEET_ID=180P707XJRdkVOVQMlfWNXTFhZfpOfqTno_ElffOnZ5w` added to `.env.local` and to the `--update-env-vars` list in `deploy.sh` (mirrors the COOPER var). No new OAuth scope needed — `spreadsheets.readonly` is already on the workspace token from the Cooper Fund deploy.

**Untouched**: Cooper Fund, Guardian Circle, Overview, Weekly Gifts, Campaigns, Capital Campaign, all other routes. The `israel_fund_summary` table is left in place but flagged as legacy in the Database section above — safe to drop in a future cleanup.

### Feature Flags admin tab — workspace-wide promotion of testing features (2026-06-03)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00580-bcg`. Adds the second axis of the flag system shipped earlier today: per-user grants (Testing) → workspace-wide promotion (Live). One click flips a feature from "Becca's preview list" to "all users with the relevant module access," and one click rolls it back.

**Database**: `workspaces.promoted_features text[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` (migration `add_promoted_features_to_workspaces`).

**Helper**: `canSeeTestingFeature(key, testingFeatures, promotedFeatures)` in `lib/testingFeatures.ts` — unions the per-user list with the workspace-wide promoted list. Gated components import this instead of `array.includes()`-checking both arrays manually.

**Session plumbing** (parallel to the Sprint 5 testing_features wiring):
- `app/api/auth/session/route.ts` SELECTs `promoted_features` on the workspace row and adds it to the `__session` cookie as `promoted_features: string[]`.
- `app/api/auth/session/workspace/route.ts` echoes it.
- `lib/auth.ts` AuthSession gained `promotedFeatures?: string[]`.
- `AuthProvider.tsx` `WorkspaceContextType` gained `promotedFeatures: string[]` (init `[]` on every reset path).

**Admin API** — `app/api/admin/feature-flags/route.ts`:
- `GET` returns `{ features: Array<{ key, module, label, description, isLive }> }`. `isLive` = workspace.promoted_features includes the key. Admin-only.
- `PATCH { key, isLive }` validates the key is in `TESTING_FEATURES`, does a read-modify-write on `workspaces.promoted_features` (idempotent — adds if missing / removes if present), returns the refreshed feature list. Admin-only.

**Admin UI** — `app/admin/permissions/page.tsx`:
- 4th tab "Feature Flags" alongside Users / School Settings / Integrations.
- Table layout: `FEATURE / MODULE / STATUS / TOGGLE`.
  - **Feature**: label + muted description.
  - **Module**: module-keyed pill (development green / admissions blue / absences amber / lever purple / simchas pink / projects indigo / home slate; falls back to slate).
  - **Status**: green badge "✅ Live — visible to all [module] users" or amber "🧪 Testing — grant per user in Permissions".
  - **Toggle**: green "Promote to Live" → confirm dialog "This will make [feature] visible to all users with [module] access. Continue?" → PATCH. "Move back to Testing" demotes directly (no confirm, since it's reversible to a state the user already trusts).
- Lazy fetch on first tab activation. Optimistic updates with rollback + success/error toasts.
- Empty state when `TESTING_FEATURES` is empty: "Add entries to `lib/testingFeatures.ts` to manage them here."

**`DevelopmentPage.tsx`** — switched to the shared helper:
```ts
const canSeeOverview = canSeeTestingFeature(
  'development_overview', testingFeatures, promotedFeatures,
);
```

The tab-filter `TABS` memo unions both arrays the same way. Promoting `development_overview` to Live in the Feature Flags tab now lights it up for every user with `development` module access — no per-user grants needed.

**Pattern when shipping a new test feature** (updated):
1. Add a `{ key, module, label, description }` entry to `TESTING_FEATURES` in `lib/testingFeatures.ts`.
2. Gate the UI with `canSeeTestingFeature(key, testingFeatures, promotedFeatures)`.
3. Grant individuals via the 🧪 Testing & Preview section in Users tab (per-user testing).
4. When ready for everyone, hit Feature Flags → Promote to Live.

### Testing & Preview feature-flag system (2026-06-03)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00578-vf9`. New per-user opt-in flag system for in-development features. First user: the Development Overview tab (Sprint 5) — invisible to anyone Becca hasn't explicitly granted access to.

**Database**: `workspace_members.testing_features text[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` (migration `add_testing_features_to_workspace_members`) + a GIN index for `ANY(...)` membership checks. Becca's row has `{development_overview}` set; everyone else defaults to `{}`.

**Registry**: `lib/testingFeatures.ts` exports `TESTING_FEATURES: TestingFeature[]` and `getTestingFeaturesForModule(module)`. Each entry has `{ key, module, label, description }`. Adding a new entry there makes it appear in the admin permissions UI's "🧪 Testing & Preview" section the same minute. Removing an entry hides it from the admin UI but does NOT clear the array values stored on existing rows (so leftover keys are harmless and re-adding a feature later doesn't lose grants).

**Session plumbing**:
- `app/api/auth/session/route.ts` SELECTs `testing_features` from `workspace_members` and adds it to the `__session` cookie payload as `testing_features: string[]`.
- `app/api/auth/session/workspace/route.ts` echoes it back from the cookie so `AuthProvider` can populate context.
- `lib/auth.ts` `AuthSession` interface gained `testingFeatures?: string[]` (server-side `getAuthSession()` consumers can check it for sensitive preview endpoints).
- `AuthProvider.tsx` `WorkspaceContextType` gained `testingFeatures: string[]`, populated from the workspace API response. Reset paths (sign-out / no-workspace) initialize to `[]`.

**Client gating** — `DevelopmentPage.tsx`:
- `ALL_TABS` now carries an optional `testingKey` per entry. `TABS` (the actually-rendered list) is `ALL_TABS.filter(t => !('testingKey' in t) || testingFeatures.includes(t.testingKey))`.
- `readInitialTab()` defaults to `'overview'` only when `canSeeOverview` is true; otherwise `'weekly-gifts'`. If a user lands on `?tab=overview` without the flag, the second `useEffect` snaps them to `weekly-gifts`. The Overview button never appears in the tab bar for ungated users — they don't even see that something is gated.

**Admin UI** — `app/admin/permissions/page.tsx`:
- Each member row gained a "🧪 Testing & Preview" collapsible (closed by default; small "N on" pill in the header when any flag is set). Expanded: features grouped by module (`development`, etc.), each with a checkbox + label + description. Subtitle: "Features in development. Only grant access to users actively helping validate."
- Optimistic `toggleTestingFeature()` PATCHes the member row; rolls back on failure.

**Admin API** — `app/api/admin/workspace-members/[id]/route.ts`:
- PATCH accepts `testing_features?: string[]` with validation: must be an array of strings; dedup + trim before write. Same admin-only auth as the existing fields.
- `GET /api/admin/workspace-members` (list) now SELECTs and returns `testing_features` so the UI can render current state without a per-member follow-up.

**OverviewTab amber beta banner removed** — gating now lives at the tab level, so the in-page disclaimer is no longer needed for the audience that can see the tab at all.

**Pattern for new test features**: (1) Add a `{ key, module, label, description }` entry to `TESTING_FEATURES` in `lib/testingFeatures.ts`. (2) In the gated component, read `useWorkspace().testingFeatures` and conditionally render. (3) Becca opens the member row in the permissions page and checks the box for whoever should test.

### Development Overview: remove segment YoY badges (2026-06-03)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00574-zqg`. The `DeltaBadge` on each segment card in `OverviewTab.tsx` was producing misleading numbers — Veracross constituent roles shift year-over-year (a parent becomes a Parent-of-Alumni when their youngest graduates, a constituent reclassifies as Faculty when hired, etc.), so a current-snapshot role can't be back-applied to last year's gift bucket. Section now shows role name + raised + donor count only. Subtitle updated to "Each donor counted once by current primary role · YoY segment comparison omitted — roles shift annually in Veracross".

Headline cards (Total Raised, Total Donors, Lapsed, New Donors) keep their YoY deltas — those are fund-level / unique-id-level totals that don't depend on role classification. Campaign Giving by Fund table also unchanged — fund identity is stable across years.

### Development: Overview tab as default landing page (Sprint 5, 2026-06-03)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00570-9rz`. New default tab on the Development section. Replaces Weekly Gifts as the landing page (Weekly Gifts is still the 2nd tab, untouched). Four sections: headline cards, segment cards by role, campaign giving YoY table, lapsed-donor collapsible list.

**`POST → GET /api/development/overview`** (new): single self-contained route. Module-gated, impersonation-aware (`getEffectiveWorkspaceId`). One paginated read across the FY25–FY26 window (`gift_type IN [1,2]`, soft-credit excluded) — ~13 pages for SAR. Then chunked `IN(...)` lookups against `constituents_cache` (500 IDs at a time) to attach role per donor. All aggregation in memory; no RPCs or materialized views. Response shape:

```
{
  headline: { raisedFY26, donorsFY26, raisedFY25, donorsFY25 },
  segments: [ { role, raisedFY26, donorsFY26, raisedFY25, donorsFY25 } × 6 ],
  campaigns: [ { fund, raisedFY26, raisedFY25 } ... sorted by FY26 desc ],
  lapsed: { count, totalLastYearDonors, donors: [{ constituent_id, name, role, lastAmount, lastDate } × 100] }
}
```

Notes:
- FY26 = 2025-09-01 → 2026-08-31; FY25 = 2024-09-01 → 2025-08-31 — hardcoded constants at the top of the route (year roll each summer).
- All six roles render in `segments` even when raised = 0 so the card grid layout stays stable.
- Lapsed list capped at 100 rows sorted by last gift amount desc; full `count` is unrestricted. Donors missing from `constituents_cache` (stale sync) default to `role: 'Other'`.

**`app/components/development/OverviewTab.tsx`**:
- Section 1: 4 headline stat cards — Total Raised FY26 (blue top border) with vs-FY25 delta badge; Total Donors FY26 (slate); Lapsed Donors (red, with red progress bar `count / totalLastYearDonors` toward 0); New Donors (green, derived client-side as `donorsFY26 - (totalLastYearDonors - lapsed.count)`).
- Section 2: 6 segment cards in a responsive grid (2 / 3 / 6 cols by breakpoint, `overflow-x-auto` + `min-w-[600px]` on small screens). Each card has role-keyed top border + YoY delta badge: Parent blue, Grandparent purple, Parents of Alumni orange, Alumni green, Faculty amber, Other slate.
- Section 3: Campaign Giving table — Fund / FY26 / FY25 / Change (dollar delta + signed %), green/red on direction, totals row, em-dash when 0.
- Section 4: Lapsed Donors collapsible (closed by default). Expanded: Name / Role / Last Gift / Last Gift Date. Role pill colors match the segment cards. "Showing top 100" note when `lapsed.count > 100`.
- Shared `DeltaBadge` component handles the `prior === 0` edge by returning a dash (no false +∞%).
- Loading: `<ShimmerStatCards count={4}>` + `<ShimmerCards count={6}>`.

**`DevelopmentPage.tsx`**:
- `TABS` array gained `{ key: 'overview', label: 'Overview' }` as the first entry. Capital Campaign moved to the end (where it was a fallback empty-state anyway).
- `DevelopmentTabKey` now includes `'overview'`. `readInitialTab()` defaults to `'overview'` when no `?tab=` deep link is present (was `'weekly-gifts'`).
- `CampaignsTab`'s `onSwitchTab` cast widened to `DevelopmentTabKey`. CampaignsTab's prop type is narrower so its only call site (`onSwitchTab('guardian-circle')`) is still typed correctly.

**Untouched**: all existing development tabs and their routes (Weekly Gifts, Campaigns, Capital Campaign, Guardian Circle, Cooper Fund, Israel Fund). This is purely additive.

### Guardian Circle: constituents_cache sync — live roles + child grades (2026-06-03)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00568-25k`. Lights up the Sprint 4 role pill + grade chips + 🚩 aging-out flag with real data. Previously the GC route returned `role: 'Other'` and `grades: []` for everyone because `gifts_cache.primary_development_role` only carried `'Household'`/`'Organization'`. This deploy adds a separate enrichment cache populated from Veracross's constituents + students endpoints.

**New Supabase table `constituents_cache`** (migration `create_constituents_cache`):
```sql
CREATE TABLE constituents_cache (
  id uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  workspace_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES workspaces(id),
  constituent_id bigint NOT NULL,
  role text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'Other',
  grades int[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',
  household_id bigint,
  roles_raw text,
  synced_at timestamptz DEFAULT now(),
  UNIQUE(workspace_id, constituent_id)
);
```
- RLS enabled with `workspace_isolation` policy (caller's workspace_id from `workspace_members` lookup via `auth.jwt()->>'email'`). All app reads go through `supabaseAdmin` (service role bypasses RLS) so the policy is the security net for any future client-side reads.
- Two indexes: by workspace_id, by (workspace_id, constituent_id).
- `roles_raw` preserves the original `person.roles` string so future parser tweaks can be backfilled without a fresh Veracross fetch.

**New sync function `lib/syncConstituents.ts`**:
- Pulls **two Veracross OAuth tokens in parallel** — development (`VERACROSS_DEVELOPMENT_CLIENT_ID/SECRET`, scope `development.constituents:list/read`) and admissions (`VERACROSS_ADMISSIONS_CLIENT_ID/SECRET`, scope `students:list/read`).
- Fetches **`/v3/development/constituents`** (all pages, parallel batches of 5 after page 1) and **`/v3/students`** (all pages) concurrently. SAR returns ~9,650 constituents + ~1,730 students; ~3 minutes for the full sync.
- Builds `householdToGrades: Map<household_id, grade_level[]>` from the student set (skipping students with past `exit_date`).
- For each constituent: `role = parseRoleFromTags(person.roles)`, `grades = householdToGrades.get(household.id) ?? []`.
- Upserts in batches of 500 with `onConflict: 'workspace_id,constituent_id'`.

**Role parser `parseRoleFromTags()`** — priority `Parent > Grandparent > Parents of Alumni > Alumni > Faculty > Other`. The production `person.roles` string uses **both** the full word `"Parent"` and the abbreviation `"Prnt"`; first deploy missed the full-word form and misclassified ~1,880 current parents as "Parents of Alumni". Fixed parser recognizes both, treats current Parent as priority winner even when "Prnt of Alum" coexists (active + alumni kids → still a Parent today), and matches both `"Faculty"` (full word) and `"Staff/Fac"` (abbreviation). See file comments for the full tag vocabulary observed in the diagnostic.

**Two new internal routes**:
- `POST /api/development/sync-constituents` — user-session-authed; same module-gating as `sync-gifts`.
- `POST /api/development/sync-constituents-internal` — shared-secret-authed for the Cloud Function. **Added to `middleware.ts` matcher exclusions** so the secret-only auth path works (mirrors `sync-gifts-internal`).
- Both call `syncConstituentsForWorkspace(workspaceId)`. Heads-up: the sync runs ~3 minutes; both Firebase Hosting and Cloud Run gateways time out at 60s, but the backend keeps running and completes. For manual triggers, hit the Cloud Run URL directly (`https://ssrrbkcmdcenter-429508710310.us-east1.run.app/...`) and verify completion via `gcloud run services logs read | grep "SYNC CONSTITUENTS"`.

**Nightly schedule** (`functions/src/index.ts`): chained right after `sync-gifts-internal` in the existing `runGiftsSyncForAllWorkspaces` loop. Independent try/catch so a constituents failure doesn't poison the gifts sync (the GC route falls back to 'Other'/[] gracefully when the cache is stale).

**`guardian-circle/route.ts` enrichment**:
- After `buildFundsFromGifts`, batch-queries `constituents_cache` for the GC constituent_ids (one `IN(...)` lookup).
- Overlays `role` + `grades` + `agingOut = grades.includes(8)` onto each constituent.
- Removed the inert `mapVcRoleToTag()` helper and the hardcoded `grades: []` default from Sprint 4.

**`ConstituentTable.tsx` + `GuardianCirclePage.tsx`**: `'Parents of Alumni'` added to the role union and the pill-class maps with `bg-orange-50 text-orange-700` styling (both row pill and sidebar pill stay in sync).

**Initial sync results (SAR workspace, 2026-06-03)** — 9,650 constituents synced:
- Other 4,870 · Grandparent 1,533 · Parents of Alumni 1,522 · Parent 933 · Alumni 712 · Faculty 80.
- 911 constituents have ≥1 current student in their household (grade chips render).
- 89 constituents flagged aging-out (8th grade child in household).

### Guardian Circle: fix "person record not found" link + drop orgs from donor count (2026-06-02)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00554-mgp`. Two Heidi/Leora bugs from the master TODO.

**FIX 1 — Veracross link "person record not found"**:
- `ConstituentTable.tsx` was building `axiom.veracross.com/sar/#/detail/person/{c.donorId}/273-general`, where `c.donorId` is the Veracross `constituent_id` (not a `person_id`). The `/detail/person/` path expects a person_id, hence the 404.
- Switched both link locations (rows + drilldown header) to the type-aware path that `WeeklyGiftsTab.tsx:597` already uses successfully:
  - persons/households → `axiom.veracross.com/sar/#/detail/development-constituent/{constituent_id}/5011-general`
  - organizations → `axiom.veracross.com/sar/#/detail/organization-constituent/{constituent_id}/5011-general`
- `fundraising-goals/route.ts` `Constituent` interface gained `constituentType: 'person' | 'organization'`, derived from `primaryDevelopmentRole` ('Organization' → 'organization', else 'person'). `ConstituentTable.Constituent` also gained an optional `constituentType?` so older cached responses don't break compilation.
- **Deferred** (added to master TODO): the spec asked for the new `app.veracross.com/sar/[person_id]/273/general` URL. That format requires `person_id`, which is not currently in `gifts_cache` and not returned by Veracross's `/v3/development/gifts` endpoint. Adopting it would require a new constituents-sync layer; out of scope for this fix.

**FIX 2 — Donor count inflated ~250 vs. real ~166**:
- Org constituents (foundations, DAFs) were counting as separate donors alongside the individual who gave through them. Hard-credit dollars on org rows are legitimate, but treating both the org and its principal as two "donors" inflates the headline metric.
- `guardian-circle/route.ts` summary mode (`?view=summary`) now **bypasses the `guardian_circle_summary` RPC entirely** — that RPC's `donor_count` column has no org filter and would still inflate. The manual aggregation path runs every time; for SAR's ~250-row Guardian Circle gift set this is a single paginated query (~150ms).
- The manual count adds `r.primary_development_role !== 'Organization'` before incrementing `donorSetNonOrg`. `totalRaised` and `outstandingTotal` are **unchanged** — those still include org dollars (real money the school received).
- Full mode (no `?view=summary`) post-filters the constituents list: `donorCount = constituents.filter(c => c.constituentType !== 'organization').length`. The constituents array still includes org rows so the table shows them.
- **If `guardian_circle_summary` RPC is later revived**, its donor-count CTE must add the matching filter — flagged in an inline comment in the route. Until then, the manual path is canonical.

### Cooper Fund: event-name consolidation + Sep 1 FY confirmation (2026-06-02)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00552-l76`. Diagnostic pass on the Money In query confirmed the FY filter is already correct (`fyStart='2025-09-01'`, `fyEnd='2026-08-31'`, `fyLabel='FY26'`, `gift_type IN [1, 2]`) — no backend change. Raw query returned 190 rows summing to $423,840 across 15 unique events. Cooper Purim 25 is **not** in the in-FY data (raised = $0), so no remap needed for it.

**Frontend consolidation** added in `CooperFundTab.tsx`:

```ts
const EVENT_NAME_MAP: Record<string, string> = {
  'israel gap year scholarships':              'M Schreck Fund / Israel Gap Year Scholarship',
  'm schreck b ball tournament':               'M Schreck Fund / Israel Gap Year Scholarship',
  'schreck fund israel gap year scholarships': 'M Schreck Fund / Israel Gap Year Scholarship',
  'm schreck fund/ israel gap year scholarship': 'M Schreck Fund / Israel Gap Year Scholarship',
  'general / undesignated': 'Cooper 25-26',
  'cooper yahrzeit':        'Cooper 25-26',
};
const EVENTS_TO_REMOVE = ['education sayeret matkal soldier'];
function normalizeEventName(name: string): string | null { ... }
```

`normalizeEventName` runs over both `moneyIn` (Veracross) and `data.disbursementsByEvent` (Google Sheet) before the `displayByKey` / `raisedByKey` / `disbursedByKey` maps are built. Returns `null` → row skipped; otherwise the canonical display name is used as the key, and amounts from variant names sum into a single bucket.

**Effect**:
- "M Schreck Fund / Israel Gap Year Scholarship" now consolidates the Veracross literal (`'M Schreck Fund/ Israel Gap Year Scholarship'`, $46,683) + the B Ball Tournament line ($50) + any future spelling variants.
- "Cooper 25-26" general bucket (the General Fund summary card) absorbs Cooper Yahrzeit ($60,599) and General / Undesignated ($360) — those no longer appear as separate chart bars or table rows.
- "Education Sayeret Matkal Soldier" is dropped entirely (misclassified — belongs to Israel Fund, not Cooper).

**Added entry beyond the literal spec**: `'m schreck fund/ israel gap year scholarship'` (no space before slash). The user spec listed three lowercase-variant keys but the Veracross canonical spelling — which holds $46k of real money — wasn't among them; without the extra entry, the variants would have rolled up under one name and the real money under a near-identical second name. Noted inline in the file comments.

**Important context for next pass**: even after these remaps, total raised on the page may still not match Leora's $350,294 ground truth. The diagnostic showed total raised pre-normalization was $423,840 — after subtracting Education Sayeret Matkal Soldier (not in the top 15, value unknown but small) and rolling Cooper Yahrzeit + General into Cooper 25-26 (zero-sum, just shifts buckets), the total stays ~$423k. The remaining $73k gap vs Leora's $350k is **not** an event-name issue; it's likely a definitional one (which gift types Leora is counting, soft-credit treatment, or pledge vs donation handling). Next diagnostic pass should compare donor names + amounts row-by-row with Leora's Veracross query export.

### Cooper Fund: live Column G disbursements from Google Sheets (2026-06-02)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00540-rkd`. The Cooper Fund tab's Raised vs. Disbursed chart + table now read disbursements live from the Cooper Reconciliation Google Sheet instead of a hardcoded constant. No more stale numbers between manual seeds.

**Wiring**:
- New env var `COOPER_RECONCILIATION_SHEET_ID` (in `.env.local` and pushed by `deploy.sh` via `--update-env-vars`). Currently `1ituaNi5OVmbx65xt_22OpHpOmMiVDh2jqls0bDfPoqA`.
- `app/api/auth/gmail-consent/route.ts` scope list gained `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/spreadsheets.readonly`. **Users connected before this deploy need to reconnect via Settings → Integrations → Gmail Reconnect before the Sheets read works for them** — the existing refresh tokens don't carry the new scope. Until that reconnect happens, `disbursementsByEvent` returns `[]` and the table falls back to "no disbursement rows" gracefully.
- `app/api/development/cooper-fund/route.ts` gained `fetchCooperDisbursementsByEvent(accessToken)`. Fetches `'Master Sheet'!A:H` via `https://sheets.googleapis.com/v4/spreadsheets/{id}/values/{range}` using the workspace `gmail_refresh_token` (`getGoogleAccessToken(wsId)`). Parses M/D/YYYY dates and `$1,234.56`-style amounts. Filters: Column G starts with `APL:` (case-insensitive), Column E credit > 0, date inside Sep 1 2025 – Aug 31 2026 (FY26 window hardcoded — bump for FY27), payee/check don't contain "VOID", payee doesn't start with Transfer/Wire/Void checks/Transfer to Operating. Strips the `APL:` prefix, groups by event name, sorts by amount desc. Returns `[]` on any failure (missing env var, no token, API non-2xx, exception). All failure paths log under the `[COOPER SHEETS]` prefix.
- `app/components/development/CooperFundTab.tsx` — removed the hardcoded `DISBURSEMENTS_BY_EVENT_FY26` constant. The chart + table now consume `data.disbursementsByEvent || []`. Also fixed two carry-over bugs from the prior incomplete deploy: table Total row referenced undefined `totalRaisedMatched` (now `totalRaisedTable`) and used Column H's `totalDisbursed` instead of the table's own `totalDisbursedTable`. Footnote updated to "Disbursement amounts read live from the Cooper Reconciliation spreadsheet (Column G). Raised amounts from Veracross."
- `CooperData` interface gained optional `disbursementsByEvent?: { name: string; amount: number }[]`.

**Failure mode**: clean. If the Sheets API call fails (token revoked, scope missing, sheet permission revoked, sheet renamed, network error), the route logs the cause and returns `disbursementsByEvent: []`. The chart shows raised-only bars; the table shows "—" in the Disbursed column. The rest of the page (stat cards, pie chart, Money In) is sourced from Supabase and unaffected.

**Why workspace token, not per-user**: the sheet is shared with the workspace owner's Google account, not individual staffers. `getGoogleAccessToken(wsId)` pulls the workspace-level `gmail_refresh_token`, which always belongs to the owner — so any user with `development` module access sees the same disbursement numbers without needing personal sheet access.

**Untouched**: stat cards (current_balance + donated_ytd + total disbursed from Column H pie still authoritative), Chart 2 pie (still on Column H `categories`), Veracross moneyIn aggregation, FY filter, gift_type filter, DonorAnnotations, all other files.
