Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00538-xf2`. Follow-up tweak to yesterday's redesign — the Raised vs. Disbursed grouped chart on `CooperFundTab.tsx` flipped from horizontal bars (category labels left, dollar axis bottom) to vertical columns (category labels bottom, dollar axis left). User preference; the horizontal layout works but stacks taller as event sprawl grows, and the column form reads more like a traditional financial chart.

- **`app/components/development/CooperFundTab.tsx`** (single file touched, only Chart 1 changed):
  - `<BarChart layout="vertical">` → removed (defaults to horizontal in Recharts).
  - **XAxis** now drives categories: `dataKey="name"`, `type="category"`, `angle={-35}`, `textAnchor="end"`, `height={80}` to give rotated labels room, `interval={0}` so every name renders (Recharts otherwise auto-skips overlapping ticks).
  - **YAxis** now drives values: `type="number"`, `tickFormatter={(v) => formatMoney(v)}` reusing the same helper as elsewhere in the file.
  - **`<ResponsiveContainer height={400}>`** — was `Math.max(rows × 46 + 30, 200)`. Vertical columns need a fixed comfortable height, not a row-count multiplier.
  - **Bar corner radius** flipped: `[0, 4, 4, 0]` (right edges, for horizontal) → `[4, 4, 0, 0]` (top edges, for vertical) — both bars.
  - **Margins** retuned: was `{ left: 160 }` (to fit horizontal y-axis labels) → `{ left: 10, right: 30, top: 10, bottom: 0 }`. Bottom margin stays 0 because the XAxis `height={80}` already reserves the space.
- **Everything else preserved** per spec — bar colors (`#4ade80` raised + `#f87171` disbursed), the custom legend row above the chart, Tooltip formatter, `overflow-x-auto` + `min-w-[500px]` mobile wrapper, both `<Bar>` series.

**Untouched**: Chart 2 pie, the 4-column table, stat cards, API route, FY filter, gift_type filter, DonorAnnotations expansion, all other files.

**Gotcha**: `interval={0}` is necessary on the XAxis — without it, Recharts auto-skips category labels when the rendered width is below its overlap threshold, which would hide some bars' x-axis labels at narrow viewports (the bars still render, just the labels disappear). The `overflow-x-auto` + `min-w-[500px]` wrapper guarantees enough horizontal room that the rotated labels at `angle={-35}` don't truncate.

### CooperFundTab redesign: Raised vs. Disbursed grouped bar + disbursement pie + 4-column table (2026-06-02)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00536-qjh`. Full rebuild of the Cooper Fund tab body (the three stat cards + API route stay untouched). Reuses the same `/api/development/cooper-fund` response — no backend change — but reorganizes the chart story around two data sources that don't actually align name-for-name.

**Source-of-truth distinction (important for future work)**:
- **`cooper_fund_categories.categories` jsonb** — Column H broad reporting categories with shape `{ name, amount }`. 10 entries in the live SAR FY26 snapshot (Getzel Camp Grants, Food & Housing Assistance, Medical & Mental Health Care, Pesach, Purim, Misc., Bereaved Family Care, Student Services, Schreck Fund Scholarships, Bar/Bat Mitzvah Support). Manually seeded; the `categories` array has no `raised` field and no event-level subdivision.
- **`gifts_cache.event`** (post `stripPrefix`) — Veracross event-level inflow tags, closer to Column G. Live SAR events include "Cooper 25-26", "Pesach for SAR Families", "Cooper Yahrzeit", "M Schreck Fund/ Israel Gap Year Scholarship", etc.

**Naming overlap is sparse** — only "Pesach" (Column H) has a fuzzy cousin in "Pesach for SAR Families" (Veracross event), and even that isn't an exact match. Per spec we do NOT fuzzy-match. The result: most table rows show "—" in the RAISED column, and a footnote explains the gap.

**`app/components/development/CooperFundTab.tsx`** rewritten (single file changed):

- **Stat cards unchanged in markup** but two color swaps:
  - `{fiscal_year} Donated` value: `text-slate-900` → **`text-green-600`** (per spec — green for incoming money).
  - `Total Disbursed` value: `text-slate-900` → **`text-red-600`** (per spec — red for outgoing).
  - `Current Balance` was already green. Stays.
- **Chart 1: "Raised vs. Disbursed · FY26"** — grouped horizontal Recharts BarChart:
  - Build `displayByKey` / `raisedByKey` / `disbByKey` maps keyed on `name.toLowerCase().trim()`. Union of all unique names across both sources, each row carries `raised` and `disbursed` independently. Sorted by `Math.max(raised, disbursed)` descending so the tallest bars float to the top.
  - Two `<Bar>` series — `dataKey="raised"` filled `#4ade80` (green-400), `dataKey="disbursed"` filled `#f87171` (red-400). Not stacked.
  - Custom legend row above the chart (Recharts' default legend reflows awkwardly inside a `ResponsiveContainer`).
  - Dynamic height: `Math.max(rows × 46 + 30, 200)`. Wrapped in `overflow-x-auto` + `min-w-[500px]`.
- **Chart 2: "Disbursements by Reporting Category"** — pie chart of the Column H categories only:
  - Warm/coral palette (`PIE_PALETTE`) — `#f87171, #fb923c, #fbbf24, #e879f9, #a78bfa, #60a5fa, #34d399, #f472b6, #94a3b8, #f97316` — cycles when more categories than colors.
  - Slice label: `${name} ${(percent * 100).toFixed(0)}%` inline (no leader lines).
  - Tooltip: dollar amount + percentage.
  - Recharts `<Legend>` underneath at `fontSize: 11`.
  - Fixed 360px height inside `ResponsiveContainer`; labels at `fontSize: 11` so they fit on mobile without breakpoint-conditional logic.
- **Table: 4 columns** — `Category | Raised | Disbursed | Remaining`:
  - Driven by `data.categories` (Column H). Sorted by `disbursed` DESC.
  - `Raised`: green (`text-green-600`) dollar amount when an exact case-insensitive name match exists in `raisedByKey`; otherwise `—` (slate-300).
  - `Disbursed`: red (`text-red-600`) dollar amount.
  - `Remaining`: `raised - disbursed` when raised matched, else `—`. Conditional color — green when positive, red when ≤ 0, slate-400 when null.
  - Total row: totals each numeric column (raised summed only over matched rows). Total Remaining colored green/red/slate by sign.
  - Click-to-expand `DonorAnnotations` row preserved per spec — same Fragment+`<tr><td colSpan={4}>` pattern, just widened from `colSpan={3}` to `colSpan={4}`.
- **Footnote** below the table (italic slate-500): "Raised amounts reflect Veracross event totals where names match disbursement categories. See chart above for full event breakdown."
- **"Data as of …" footer** moved to below the table per spec.
- **Removed**: old single-color blue disbursement bar chart + old green Money In bar chart. Their data feeds Chart 1's two series and the table now.

**Known limitation flagged for future schema work**:
- The categories array currently lacks a `raised` field at the schema level — only `name + amount`. To make Chart 1 truly accurate (Column H raised vs. Column H disbursed instead of Column G raised vs. Column H disbursed), `cooper_fund_categories.categories` would need an additional `raised: number` field per entry, manually maintained alongside `amount`. Until then, Chart 1 mixes the two granularities side-by-side; the table's RAISED column will mostly show "—" because Veracross event names like "Pesach for SAR Families" don't exactly match Column H categories like "Pesach". Documented for the next Heidi sync — could be solved either by enriching the snapshot row or by adding a Column G → Column H mapping table.

**Untouched** per spec: API route (`/api/development/cooper-fund`), FY filter logic (Sep 1 – Aug 31), gift_type IN (1, 2) hard-credit filter, `moneyInFyLabel`, DonorAnnotations expansion behavior, all other files.

**Gotchas**:
- Recharts `<Tooltip formatter={(value, name) => …}>` types value as `ValueType | undefined`. Don't annotate the parameter types — let Recharts inject them. Original `(value: number | string, name: string) => …` failed strict-mode type-check.
- The unique-name union for Chart 1 can produce a tall chart when Veracross has lots of small events (~17 distinct events in the live FY26 data). Bars stay 16px each + 30px label padding, so the chart wraps cleanly inside the overflow-x scroll. If event sprawl grows past ~30 rows, consider grouping below-threshold events into a synthetic "Other Events" bar.
- The pie chart's label-on-slice approach (no leader lines) can collide for tiny slices (<2%). At SAR's current FY26 distribution the smallest slice is "Bar/Bat Mitzvah Support" at $1,000 / ~0.2% — its label currently renders as `Bar/Bat Mitzvah Support 0%` which is correct but visually crowded. If real-world feedback flags this, switch to `labelLine={true}` + outside labels.

### Guardian Circle: 🔒 Anon pill on anonymous-gift donor rows (2026-06-01)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00534-w7s`. From Heidi Greenbaum's Development review (the Known Pending Items "anonymous gift flag on Guardian Circle donor rows" item): when a donor checks "I want to be anonymous" on an online gift form, Veracross sets `anonymous = true` on the gift row. The internal dashboard should still show the constituent name (for staff context) but staff need an at-a-glance indicator that the donor doesn't want to be publicly named.

- **`app/api/development/fundraising-goals/route.ts`**:
  - `GiftRow` interface gained `anonymous: boolean | null`.
  - `Constituent` interface gained `anonymous: boolean` (required, not optional — the helper always sets it).
  - `buildFundsFromGifts` tracks `let isAnonymous = false; if (r.anonymous === true) isAnonymous = true;` across the per-donor inner loop. **Conservative aggregation**: once a donor has flagged any gift in the rolled-up scope as anonymous, the constituent pill shows. Reasoning: even if their next gift is non-anonymous, "anonymous historically" is still a publish-with-caution signal for staff.
  - SELECT in the main paginated query (line ~211) gained `anonymous`.
- **`app/api/development/guardian-circle/route.ts`**: SELECT (line ~91) also gained `anonymous` so the row hydrates correctly. The `summary` view (line ~58 SELECT) doesn't need the field — it only returns aggregates, no constituent list.
- **`app/components/development/ConstituentTable.tsx`**:
  - `Constituent` interface gained `anonymous?: boolean` (optional on the client side so a stale browser cache holding a pre-deploy response still types cleanly).
  - **Mobile row**: donor-name `<span>` widened to `inline-flex items-center gap-1.5`; pill renders immediately after the name when `c.anonymous === true`. Pill markup matches the spec exactly: `inline-flex items-center gap-1 text-xs font-medium text-slate-500 bg-slate-100 border border-slate-200 rounded px-1.5 py-0.5` containing `🔒 Anon`.
  - **Desktop row**: pill renders inside the donor-name cell, immediately after `<span>{c.donorName}</span>` and before the existing donor-tags inline-flex. Same markup as mobile.
- **Untouched**: row click behavior, sorting, DonorAnnotations, the donor-tag pills, the amount/pledge cells, the thank-you-letter date column, all other ConstituentTable layout. Only the donor-name cell got the new conditional sibling.

**Gotchas**:
- The pill renders the same in compact and non-compact ConstituentTable modes since the donor-name `<span>` is the only injection point. Compact mode (Campaigns tab accordions) will show the pill at the same size as the non-compact mode (Guardian Circle main view) — slightly visually heavier than the surrounding `text-[10px]` donor-tags in compact mode, but the pill semantics warrant the slight emphasis.
- **`Constituent.anonymous` is required on the server type but optional on the client type.** That's deliberate — the server always emits the field (the helper sets `anonymous: isAnonymous`), but the client type tolerates a missing field for back-compat with any stale `useState`/cache that pre-dates this deploy. New code reading the field should guard with `=== true` rather than truthy check.
- The "any gift anonymous" aggregation is broader than Veracross's per-gift anonymous flag. If a donor has 5 gifts and only the first was flagged anonymous, the pill will still show. If staff need the per-gift granularity (e.g. for the donor-history panel), surface it from the gift rows themselves rather than the constituent rollup.

### Today's Schedule: timezone-aware date filter (2026-06-01)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00530-9p4`. **Bug**: timed events near the local-day boundary were rendering on the wrong day because the API route built its UTC `timeMin`/`timeMax` window from the **server's** local time (Cloud Run = UTC) rather than the user's local time, and dropped the per-event `start.timeZone` Google returns. Net effect for SAR's ET users: an 8 PM ET event on Tuesday → 00:00 UTC Wednesday → would either fall outside the Tuesday UTC window (missing) or get bucketed into Wednesday (mis-dated). DST shifts the offset by an hour but the symptom is the same.

- **`app/api/calendar/today/route.ts`** (single file touched):
  - New `DEFAULT_TIMEZONE = 'America/New_York'` constant — SAR's home timezone, used when Google omits `start.timeZone` (rare; happens on imported events) and as the default "today" reference when the client doesn't pass `?date=`.
  - New `getLocalDateString(input, timeZone)` helper — uses `Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-CA', { timeZone, year: '2-digit', month: '2-digit', day: '2-digit' })` to render a YYYY-MM-DD string in the requested zone. `en-CA` was chosen because its `Intl` output is YYYY-MM-DD, which is the same shape Google uses for all-day events' `start.date` — so a single string compare works against either timed or all-day events without further normalization.
  - **Target date**: now `dateParam ?? getLocalDateString(new Date(), DEFAULT_TIMEZONE)`. The client still passes `?date=YYYY-MM-DD` for explicit navigation; when omitted, the route computes "today in ET" rather than "today in UTC".
  - **Google window widened**: timeMin/timeMax now span `[targetDate − 1 day, targetDate + 2 days]` in UTC — a 72-hour window. Any IANA zone is within ±14 hours of UTC, so this guarantees every event whose local date is `targetLocalDate` is returned by Google; the in-memory filter then narrows precisely. Also robust across DST transitions.
  - **In-memory filter** after Google returns: timed events compare `getLocalDateString(event.start.dateTime, event.start.timeZone || DEFAULT_TIMEZONE) === targetLocalDate`; all-day events compare `event.start.date === targetLocalDate` directly (already in YYYY-MM-DD).
  - **Per-event response now includes `timeZone`** — the IANA name from `event.start.timeZone`, or `DEFAULT_TIMEZONE` fallback when Google omits it on a timed event, or `null` for all-day events. Dashboard.tsx doesn't consume the field yet but it's available for future cross-TZ rendering work.
- **Dashboard.tsx untouched** (per spec — "use judgment"). The existing `formatTime` renders via `format(parseISO(time), 'h:mm a')` which honors the offset baked into Google's dateTime string. SAR users in ET viewing ET-tagged events render correctly today; a cross-TZ user would still see browser-local time, which is acceptable until/unless someone reports it.

**Gotchas**:
- **Multi-day all-day events** (e.g. a "School Closed" event spanning 3 days) only match on their first day under the new filter because `start.date === targetLocalDate` is single-day equality. Adding range support is `start.date <= targetLocalDate && targetLocalDate < end.date`. Out of scope per the user's spec ("Use start.date directly").
- **DEFAULT_TIMEZONE fallback for Google-omitted TZ** is a stable choice for SAR but would mis-date an event a non-SAR user added to their calendar in another timezone if Google ever omitted the TZ field. In practice Google emits `start.timeZone` on virtually every event coming from a user's primary calendar; the fallback only fires on exotic imports.
- **The 72-hour window** triples the data Google returns vs the prior 24-hour window, but Google's API is rate-limited by quota units (not byte count), so this isn't a concern in practice. If the per-request payload ever becomes a perf issue, narrow to `[targetDate − 1 day at 14:00 UTC, targetDate + 1 day at 14:00 UTC]` — that's the tightest bound that still covers every IANA zone.
- **Server clock matters**: Cloud Run NTP-syncs; `getLocalDateString(new Date(), DEFAULT_TIMEZONE)` could in theory drift by seconds across the midnight ET boundary, briefly resolving "today" to the previous calendar date. Practical impact: zero — clock drift on Cloud Run is sub-millisecond.

### Bar/Bat Mitzvah iCal filter — keyword guard against shared-calendar leakage (2026-05-28)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00813-ger`. Surfaced via Heidi Greenbaum's review: the Bar/Bat Mitzvahs section on the Simchas page was showing the Moshava trip and Israel Day Parade, neither of which are simchas. Root cause: `/api/simchas` fetches the public iCal feed at `calendar.google.com/.../barbatmitzvah@saracademy.org` and passed every event through, filtering only on `(eventDate >= weekStart && eventDate <= weekEnd)`. In practice that calendar is reused by staff for general school events, so non-mitzvah entries leak in. There was zero title/category check.

- **`app/api/simchas/route.ts`** (only file touched):
  - New module-level `MITZVAH_TITLE_KEYWORDS` constant with 5 case-insensitive substrings: `'bar mitzvah'`, `'bat mitzvah'`, `"b'nei mitzvah"`, `'bnei mitzvah'`, `'bar/bat mitzvah'`. Chosen to cover the spellings staff actually use; lowercase straight quotes for the apostrophe (the iCal feed delivers SUMMARY as ASCII, no smart quotes).
  - New `isMitzvahTitle(summary)` helper — lowercases and `.some()`-tests for substring inclusion.
  - GET handler's date filter now also requires `isMitzvahTitle(event.summary)`. Events outside the date range short-circuit before the keyword check.
- **Email-based filter untouched** per spec ("only Bar/Bat Mitzvahs section"): `Dashboard.tsx:~2778` still uses `text.includes('bar mitzvah') || text.includes('bat mitzvah')` for the `bneiMitzvahInvitations` array (which feeds the Invitations sub-section on the Simchas page, sourced from emails — a different data path). The user's reported false positives (Moshava, Israel Day Parade) wouldn't have hit the email path anyway since invitation emails for those wouldn't contain "bar mitzvah" text.

**Gotchas**:
- The keyword check is on SUMMARY only, not LOCATION or DESCRIPTION. A future event titled just "Goldberg Family Celebration" would slip through (if staff omit the simcha label) but that's actually the right tradeoff — the alternative is matching every event with "mitzvah" in the description, which would catch random school assemblies that mention bar mitzvah preparation.
- Apostrophe handling: the `"b'nei mitzvah"` entry uses a straight ASCII apostrophe. If Google Calendar ever delivers a smart-quote `’` (U+2019), the substring check would miss it. The iCal export does NOT smart-quote — verified — but if SAR ever switches feed sources, normalize the apostrophes before matching.
- The list excludes things like `"bar-mitzvah"` (hyphenated), `"bnai mitzvah"` (alternate transliteration), and `"becoming a bar mitzvah"` (descriptive). If those start showing up in legitimate calendar entries, extend the list.

### Grade-edit indicator moved from table row to student drilldown pill (2026-05-28)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00526-lqt`. The Enrollment Projection By Grade table used to render a small amber pencil-icon next to any grade label whose row contained one or more manually overridden students (`override_grade !== original_grade`). The indicator was visually noisy on the table — every row could potentially carry one, with no way to see which students drove it without opening the drilldown. Moved the signal closer to the student level.

- **Removed from the projection By Grade table** (`app/components/Dashboard.tsx` lines ~8491-8495): the amber pencil-icon `<span>` that rendered when `overridesByGrade.get(gradeKey) > 0`. Grade label cell now just renders `{gradeKey}` cleanly. The `overridesByGrade` Map computation upstream was **kept** per spec ("Do not remove any underlying state or data — just the visual indicator on the table row") — the map is computed but no longer read by any JSX.
- **Added to the projection_combined drilldown student card** (line ~9764, inside the badges row): new "Grade manually edited" pill rendered alongside the existing "Moved from X" badge when `override_grade !== original_grade`. Styling per spec: `text-xs font-medium text-amber-600 bg-amber-50 border border-amber-200 rounded px-1.5 py-0.5 inline-flex items-center gap-1` with a pencil SVG (same `M15.232 5.232...` path as the removed table indicator, in `w-3 h-3` size). Both pills now wrap inside a fragment.
- **Untouched per spec**:
  - The "Moved from X" badge (existing visual element — kept alongside the new pill so the original grade is still readable in the same row).
  - The `overridesByGrade` computation block at ~8280-8288 (state/data).
  - All grade override logic: save, revert, Pisgah toggle, the `effectiveGrade` helper, the drilldown panel chrome.
  - Other drilldown variants (projection_pending / projection_pisgah / projection_incomplete / projection_waitlist / projection_leaving / non-projection grade/status/response branch) — none of them currently surface grade overrides, so the new pill was scoped to `projection_combined` only. If a future change adds override editing to those variants, mirror the pill alongside any new "Moved from X" badge there.

**Gotcha**: the `overridesByGrade` Map is now computed and discarded. Worth removing when next editing this block, but left for now to satisfy the "don't change underlying state" constraint. Lint will eventually catch it as unused — currently flagged via "ESLint disable" pragmas elsewhere in the same scope, but no new pragma was added here since `overridesByGrade` is just unused at the call site, not assigned-and-unread.

### Israel Fund Money In: exclude soft-credit gift types (2026-05-28)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00524-ltg`. Same bug, same fix as the Cooper Fund Money In deploy earlier today — the Israel Fund Money In · By Event query against `gifts_cache` was summing both hard-credit and soft-credit rows, double-counting any gift recorded against a spouse / household pair.

- **`app/api/development/israel-fund/route.ts`** (only file touched): paginated `gifts_cache` query gained `.in('gift_type', [1, 2])` alongside the existing `.eq('fundraising_activity', 'External Funds General')` and `.gt('amount', 0)` filters. Restricts to:
  - `gift_type = 1` Donation (hard credit)
  - `gift_type = 2` Pledge (hard credit)
  
  Excludes:
  - `gift_type = 3` Donation Soft-Credit
  - `gift_type = 5` Pledge Soft-Credit
  
  Comment block above the line points at the matching Cooper fix from earlier today so the two routes can be reviewed together.
- **Untouched** per spec: `israel_fund_summary` queries, initiatives table, summary stat cards, the `pledge_balance` per-event tally (which still accumulates from gift_type=2 rows only — pattern was already correct because the prior code already filtered pledge balance on gift_type=2). All client rendering in `IsraelFundTab.tsx` continues unchanged.

**Adjacent issue, NOT addressed in this fix**: pledge amount + `pledge_balance` double-attribution. For `gift_type = 2` (Pledge) rows, the route currently:
1. Sums the row's `amount` (the pledge's full face value) into the per-event `total`
2. Separately accumulates the same row's `pledge_balance` into the per-event `pledgeBalance`

Both numbers are surfaced in the Money In table. The face value covers the entire pledge commitment and the balance is the unpaid portion — so a $100K pledge with $40K paid + $60K outstanding shows correctly as "raised $100K, $60K balance." But when actual payment installments come through as `gift_type = 1` Donation rows applying against the same pledge, those amounts also land in the per-event `total`, and the pledge's $100K already counted the full commitment. Net effect: payments-against-pledges may inflate the per-event total. Known issue; deferred until a future review reconciles the payment-vs-pledge accounting model.

### Cooper Money In: exclude soft-credit gift types (2026-05-28)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00522-kld`. The Cooper Fund Money In chart was double-counting every gift that had a soft-credit counterpart. Veracross records a household pledge as two rows — one hard credit on the primary donor + one soft credit on the spouse — both at the full amount; the prior query summed both. Confirmed via the Rosen FY26 Israel Gap Year pledge: a single $15K pledge surfaced as $30K (id 225502 hard credit + id 225504 soft credit pointing at 225502).

- **`app/api/development/cooper-fund/route.ts`** — paginated `gifts_cache` query gained `.in('gift_type', [1, 2])` alongside the existing fund / fundraising_activity / FY date filters. Restricts the aggregation to:
  - `gift_type = 1` Donation (hard credit)
  - `gift_type = 2` Pledge (hard credit)
  
  And excludes:
  - `gift_type = 3` Donation Soft-Credit
  - `gift_type = 5` Pledge Soft-Credit
  
  (Same gift_type code semantics WeeklyGiftsTab already relies on for its `isSoftCredit` badge.)
- **No other changes**: disbursement card, `cooper_fund_categories` fetch, chart rendering, pagination logic, fiscal year boundary, `moneyInFyLabel`, and the by-event grouping in `stripPrefix` are all untouched.

**Expected effect**: yesterday's deploy showed ~$895K (375 rows summed across gift_types 1/2/3/5). The pre-fix breakdown was: type 1 = $325,244 (174 rows) / type 2 = $63,560 (12 rows) / type 3 = $443,111 (178 rows, soft credits) / type 5 = $63,200 (11 rows, soft credits). Today's chart should show only types 1 + 2 = **~$388K across 186 rows** for FY26.

Leora's internal Cooper Money In total is **$350,294** — the ~$38K gap vs our $388K is the methodology difference (her query likely excludes pledge gifts pending payment, or applies a different fund/event scope). The $38K delta is small enough to be acceptable as a known methodology gap rather than a bug; if it widens, revisit by comparing per-event totals against her spreadsheet.

**Gotcha**: this is the same soft-credit-dedup problem WeeklyGiftsTab solves via `hard_credit_gift_id` grouping rather than gift_type filtering (so it can show a soft-credited gift but pick whichever variant is more useful for display). For an aggregation-only Money In chart the simpler filter works because the hard-credit row already carries the full amount — but if a future Cooper view ever needs to show individual gifts (not just totals), switch to the WeeklyGiftsTab dedup pattern instead so soft-credit-only gifts (rare but possible) aren't dropped.

### iOS PWA login: indexedDBLocalPersistence in standalone mode (2026-05-28)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00520-wkd`. Fixes the "Unable to process request due to missing initial state" error iOS Add-to-Home-Screen users were hitting when signing in. **Root cause**: iOS Safari in standalone (PWA) mode partitions `sessionStorage` between the PWA window and the OAuth popup it opens — Firebase's default `browserLocalPersistence` writes auth state to sessionStorage during the popup roundtrip, and the partitioned popup can't see it on return, so the state lookup fails.

- **`app/login/page.tsx`** (only file touched):
  - Imports widened: added `setPersistence`, `browserLocalPersistence`, `indexedDBLocalPersistence` alongside the existing `GoogleAuthProvider` + `signInWithPopup`.
  - New `isIosPwa()` helper: returns true when either (a) the legacy `navigator.standalone === true` flag (still set on iOS PWAs as of iOS 18) or (b) `display-mode: standalone` media query matches AND UA contains `iPhone | iPad | iPod`. The two-condition check is defensive — older iOS releases populated one or the other; modern releases populate both.
  - **Persistence is set before** `signInWithPopup` is invoked: `setPersistence(auth, isIosPwa() ? indexedDBLocalPersistence : browserLocalPersistence)`. IndexedDB survives Safari's standalone-mode sessionStorage partition because it's keyed by origin, not by browsing context. Non-iOS users continue with the default `browserLocalPersistence` so there's zero behavior change on desktop / Android / regular Safari.
  - The actual `signInWithPopup(auth, provider)` call and everything after it (idToken fetch, session-cookie POST, router.push) are completely unchanged.
- **`app/components/Dashboard.tsx`** silent re-auth path (`signInWithPopup` at line 660 with `prompt: 'none'`) deliberately **not touched** per the prompt. That call only fires for users who are already signed in and need a fresh Google access token — by that point the Firebase auth state is already persisted, and the popup is short-lived. If iOS PWA users start reporting the same error on the silent-reauth path, mirror the `setPersistence(...indexedDBLocalPersistence)` call there.

**Why not switch persistence globally to IndexedDB**: IndexedDB has more failure modes on private-browsing and old browser builds than `browserLocalPersistence`. Keeping the default for non-iOS-PWA users avoids regressing those paths to fix a narrow iOS-specific symptom.

**Verification path for next time**: open the app from the iOS home-screen icon (not Safari), tap Sign in, complete Google auth, return to the PWA. The previous failure showed an immediate "missing initial state" error toast; the fix should now land cleanly on `/` with a valid session cookie.

### Cooper Fund FY boundary corrected: Sep 1 – Aug 31 (not Jul 1 – Jun 30) (2026-05-28)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00518-xj8`. Yesterday's deploy added an FY filter to Cooper Fund Money In using Jul 1 – Jun 30 boundaries. That's wrong for SAR — the actual SAR fiscal year is **Sep 1 – Aug 31**. Heidi flagged this on review. So FY26 = 2025-09-01 → 2026-08-31, not 2025-07-01 → 2026-06-30.

- **`app/api/development/cooper-fund/route.ts`** `getFiscalYear()` updated:
  - Pivot month: `month >= 7` → `month >= 9`
  - Date strings: `-07-01` / `-06-30` → `-09-01` / `-08-31`
  - Comment block rewritten to reflect Sep 1 – Aug 31 and to call out the distinction from the school year (which also starts Sep 1 but ends Jun 30 because students leave in June).
- **No client change**: `CooperFundTab.tsx` reads `moneyInFyLabel` verbatim. The label is still computed off `fyEndYear` (e.g. `"FY26"` for any date in Sep 2025 – Aug 2026), so the UI keeps working unchanged.
- **No other files needed touching**:
  - `app/api/absences/route.ts` `schoolYearStartIso` already uses Sep 1 as the start with a Jun 30 end. That's the **school year** (when students are physically present), not the fiscal year. Different concept, intentionally different end date — left as-is.
  - `app/api/development/draft-thank-you/route.ts` has no FY logic at all. It accepts a `date` field from the request and passes it through to the AI prompt. Left as-is.

**Effect on data**: FY26 window now covers Sep 1, 2025 → Aug 31, 2026. Compared to yesterday's deploy, this excludes the July–August 2025 portion of CY 2025 Cooper gifts (those belong to FY25 under the corrected boundary) and includes a July–August 2026 tail (which doesn't matter yet — we're still in May 2026). On the production data set, the chart total should drop slightly vs yesterday's number; the shape of the chart (events ranked by total) should be similar.

**Gotcha for future fiscal-year logic**: SAR fiscal year ≠ U.S. federal fiscal year (which is Oct 1 – Sep 30), ≠ most U.S. nonprofit calendars (which often follow Jul 1 – Jun 30 to match the school year). When adding any FY filter in a new route, copy the `getFiscalYear` helper from this route verbatim or lift it to `lib/schoolYear.ts` rather than re-deriving boundaries from scratch.

### Cooper Fund Money In: scoped to current fiscal year (2026-05-27)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00516-zg5`. Surfaced via Heidi Greenbaum's review: the Money In · By Event chart on the Cooper Fund tab was showing ~$1.39M lifetime Cooper-gift total instead of the in-FY total. Disbursement card on the same page was already FY-scoped (from the manually-seeded `cooper_fund_categories` snapshot), so the two halves of the page were measuring different time windows. Today is in FY26; the chart now shows only July 1, 2025 → June 30, 2026 (~$447K).

- **`app/api/development/cooper-fund/route.ts`**:
  - New `getFiscalYear(today)` helper: month >= 7 ⇒ start year is current calendar year, else previous. Returns `{ fyStart: 'YYYY-07-01', fyEnd: 'YYYY-06-30', fyLabel: 'FY26' }`. Label keyed off end-year to match how the disbursement snapshot's manually-set `fiscal_year` column reads.
  - `gifts_cache` paginated query gained `.gte('date', fyStart).lte('date', fyEnd)` alongside the existing `fund='Cooper'` / `fundraising_activity='Cooper'` filters. Column name confirmed against `weekly-gifts/route.ts` — it's `date` on `gifts_cache`, not `gift_date`.
  - Response includes new `moneyInFyLabel` field (string, e.g. `"FY26"`). Kept distinct from the existing `fiscal_year` field, which is the snapshot's manually-set label and can drift from the computed FY label if Heidi/Leora are slow to seed the next year's snapshot.
- **`app/components/development/CooperFundTab.tsx`**:
  - `CooperData` interface gained optional `moneyInFyLabel?: string` so any in-flight client cache from the prior deploy still types cleanly.
  - The Money In section header now reads `"Money In · By Event · FY26"` when the label is present (falls back to `"Money In · By Event"` when not).
  - **No other changes** — disbursement card, bar chart, pagination, money-in vs money-out split, and total-money-in stat are all untouched.

**Prior behavior (now fixed)**: the Money In aggregation summed every Cooper-tagged gift in `gifts_cache` regardless of date. Live data showed ~$1.39M total across April 2025 → May 2026 (348 gifts in CY 2025, 162 in CY 2026). Now scoped to FY26 (July 2025 – June 2026), which excludes the ~April–June 2025 portion of the spring 2025 gifts (those belong to FY25). Should bring the chart total close to the disbursement snapshot's `donated_ytd` value.

**Gotchas**:
- The `getFiscalYear` helper is duplicated in spirit with the `schoolYearStartIso` / `quarterForDate` helpers from `app/api/absences/route.ts` — different start month (July vs September) so they're not literally the same function, but if a third FY/SY helper lands, lift them all to `lib/schoolYear.ts`.
- `moneyInFyLabel` is computed at request time from the server's `new Date()`. If the server clock somehow drifts across year-end, the label could disagree with the disbursement snapshot's `fiscal_year` for a few minutes. Not worth defending against — Cloud Run clocks are NTP-synced.
- The Israel Fund route (`app/api/development/israel-fund/route.ts`) likely has the same all-time-vs-FY mismatch on its Money In chart. Out of scope for this fix, but worth checking next time the Israel Fund tab gets touched.

### Notes & Tags on every admissions drilldown variant (2026-05-26)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00514-s5b`. Extends the per-student notes + tags panel — previously only in the `projection_combined` (By Grade row click) drilldown — to every other admissions drilldown variant that shows individual student cards.

- **6 variants updated** in `app/components/Dashboard.tsx`, each one's student card map rewritten to match the existing `projection_combined` pattern (chevron in header, full-card `cursor-pointer` + `onClick` toggle, `e.stopPropagation()` on the Veracross "open in" link, conditional Notes & Tags block below the badges):
  - `projection_leaving` — not-re-enrolling students; `source = 're'` hard-coded since all rows are from `reEnrollmentsData`. Full name built inline from `${s.first_name} ${s.last_name}`.
  - `projection_pending` — pending-review applicants; `source = 'new'` hard-coded since all rows are from `admissionsData`.
  - `projection_waitlist` — waitlisted applicants; `source = 'new'`.
  - `projection_pisgah` — mixed new + re-enrollment; the existing `rows` array already carries `r.source` per row, used directly in `studentKey`.
  - `projection_incomplete` — same mixed shape as pisgah; uses `row.source`.
  - **Non-projection branch** (handles `grade`, `projection_grade`, `status`, `response`) — applicants from `admissionsData`; `source = 'new'`. Chevron moved into the right-side header group next to the existing `application_date` label so the layout stays tight.
- **Shared state, not per-variant**: all 6 variants reuse the existing `admissionsDrilldownTags` (workspace-wide tag bulk-fetch), `loadedAdmissionsTagsRef` (one-shot session gate), `updateAdmissionsTag` (callback), and `expandedAdmissionsStudent` (which card is open). Safe because the drilldown panels are mutually exclusive — only one variant renders at a time, gated by `admissionsDrilldown.type`. `expandedAdmissionsStudent` is already reset to `null` when `admissionsDrilldown` changes (line ~1602's useEffect), so cross-variant collisions can't happen.
- **`studentKey` format** is `${source}-${personId}` for every variant. Same scheme as `projection_combined` — used both for the open-state toggle and for the `${source}` part of any future cross-variant indexing.
- **`projection_category` was already covered**: the render branch at line ~9583 handles both `projection_category` and `projection_combined` in the same code block, so the existing annotations pattern there serves both. No change needed.
- **Untouched**: `DonorAnnotations.tsx`, the donor-notes / donor-tags API routes, `ADMISSIONS_TAG_DEFS` constant, the variant-data computation logic (filters, sorts) at the top of each branch. Only the JSX `.map()` bodies changed.

**Gotchas**:
- The `projection_pending` / `projection_waitlist` / `projection_leaving` variants hardcode `source` because the underlying data source determines it (admissions vs re-enrollment). If a future variant mixes both sources (like `projection_pisgah` already does), follow the pisgah/incomplete pattern of carrying `source` on each row instead.
- The `studentKey` uniquely identifies a person within the panel, but the same person could appear in two variants (e.g. a Pisgah-flagged incomplete applicant). The shared `expandedAdmissionsStudent` state means switching from one drilldown variant to another keeps the same person's panel "remembered" — but since the drilldown panel itself unmounts on type change, the new variant's card will start collapsed. This is correct behavior (existing useEffect resets the state).
- Six variants now duplicate ~30 lines of identical wrapper JSX. If a 7th or 8th variant gets added, factor into a small helper component (`<AdmissionsStudentCard>`) instead of copy-pasting. The current 6 are close enough to in-place edits that splitting them now would only add indirection without payoff.

### DailyAnnouncementsCard: mobile branch (iframe → direct link) (2026-05-26)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00512-klq`. Mobile Safari's ITP blocks the Google Docs iframe (the embedded viewer relies on Google's third-party cookies, which Safari rejects on cross-site iframes), so on viewports < 768px we now skip the iframe entirely and render a centered "Open Daily Announcements →" link button instead.

- **URL constants restructured** to a single source of truth: `DOC_URL` is the canonical `/edit` link (used by the mobile button and by the desktop fallback's "Open in Google Docs →"); `EMBED_URL` is derived from it via a small replace (`/edit.*$/ → /pub?embedded=true`). The `EMBED_URL = DOC_URL.includes('/pub') ? DOC_URL : DOC_URL.replace(...)` form lets a future maintainer swap `DOC_URL` to a pre-published URL without breaking anything.
- **`isMobile` detection**: one-shot `useEffect` that calls `setIsMobile(window.innerWidth < 768)` on mount. No resize listener — mobile users don't typically change viewport mid-session, and adding the listener adds cleanup overhead for a negligible UX gain. SSR and the first client render both use `isMobile = false`, so desktop never sees a flash; mobile sees the iframe for a single render before the effect flips state to `true`. Acceptable since the iframe wouldn't have had time to load anything in that window.
- **Mobile branch JSX**: centered `flex flex-col items-center justify-center py-12 gap-4` block with the file-text SVG icon (same path as the card header), a slate-500 message "Daily Announcements can't be embedded on mobile.", and a blue `bg-blue-600 hover:bg-blue-700 active:bg-blue-800` button text "Open Daily Announcements →" that targets `_blank` on `DOC_URL`.
- **Desktop branch unchanged in behavior**: iframe + 10s timeout + error/Retry fallback from the prior deploy all preserved. The timeout `useEffect` got gated on `!isMobile` so it doesn't arm uselessly on the mobile path (where the iframe never renders).
- **The `loadedRef` ref pattern stays as defense-in-depth on desktop** per spec — only the mobile branch removes the iframe dependency, not the desktop fallback machinery.

**Gotchas**:
- The 768px breakpoint matches Tailwind's `md:`. iPad portrait (768px) lands on the desktop branch — intentional, since iPad Safari handles the Google Docs iframe correctly. If we ever see ITP-style failures on iPad, bump to `< 1024` (i.e. `lg:` breakpoint).
- The breakpoint is checked **once on mount**. A user who resizes their desktop browser narrower than 768px after mount won't see the mobile branch until they reload. Acceptable for the on-mount-only `home` page — no router transitions stay on this card without remounting.
- The mobile SVG icon uses the same path as the card header (file-text). Matches visually but doesn't carry the slate-200 "error" connotation of the desktop fallback's exclamation-circle. That's intentional — the mobile branch is the normal path on mobile, not an error state.
- `EMBED_URL`'s `.replace(/\/edit.*$/, ...)` regex assumes `DOC_URL` ends with `/edit` (with or without query string). If someone swaps `DOC_URL` to end with `/preview` or `/view`, the regex won't match and `EMBED_URL === DOC_URL`. The desktop iframe would then load the unmodified `DOC_URL` — likely still works (it's a valid Google Docs URL) but would lose the "embedded" header-stripping behavior. Worth a defensive `.replace(/\/(edit|preview|view).*$/, ...)` if this gets edited.

### DailyAnnouncementsCard: published-embed URL + iframe error fallback (2026-05-26)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00510-qnd`. Two fixes to the Daily Announcements card on `/home`.

- **Embed URL switched** from `/preview` to `/pub?embedded=true`. Previously: `https://docs.google.com/document/d/<id>/preview` (the in-browser doc preview format). Now: `https://docs.google.com/document/d/<id>/pub?embedded=true`. Refactored to compute both URLs from a single `GDOC_ID` constant + a sibling `GDOC_DIRECT_URL` (`/edit`) used by the fallback's "Open in Google Docs →" link. **Important**: `/pub?embedded=true` requires the doc to have been explicitly published via File → Share → Publish to web. If it hasn't been, the iframe fails and the fallback takes over.
- **Error + timeout fallback** added, mirroring the `ThisWeekCard` pattern exactly:
  - New `iframeError` state (boolean), `iframeKey` state (retry counter), `loadedRef` (ref instead of state because the 10s timeout closure needs the latest value without re-running the effect), and `timeoutRef` for the 10s timer.
  - `useEffect` keyed on `iframeKey` resets `loadedRef.current = false` + `iframeError = false`, then arms a `setTimeout` that sets `iframeError = true` if the iframe hasn't fired `onLoad` within 10 seconds.
  - `handleLoad` flips the ref + clears the timeout. `handleRetry` increments `iframeKey` so the iframe remounts via React key change, which re-runs the effect for a fresh 10s window.
  - When `iframeError` is true, replaces the iframe with a centered card matching `ThisWeekCard`'s failed-state visuals (same SVG, same `text-slate-500 text-sm` message, same Retry button + secondary link side-by-side). Message text: "Daily Announcements couldn't load." Link text: "Open in Google Docs →" (target `_blank`, opens `GDOC_DIRECT_URL`).
- **Iframe onLoad / onError handlers** wired up: `onLoad={handleLoad}` and `onError={() => setIframeError(true)}`. The iframe also gets `key={iframeKey}` so Retry triggers a real remount, not just a re-render.

**Untouched**: tab logic in `app/home/page.tsx`, the DailyAnnouncementsCard header (icon + title + Today's Folder button + collapse chevron), and the rest of the home page. The collapsed-state transition (`maxHeight`/`opacity`) was kept verbatim so the card animates the same way it always has.

**Gotchas**:
- If the doc hasn't been Published to web, the embed URL will return a "You need permission" page that may or may not fire `onError`. The 10s timeout handles the silent-fail case; the explicit `onError` handles the loud-fail case. Either way the user lands on the fallback. To verify the embed works, open the doc → File → Share → Publish to web → publish (then any reload picks it up).
- `loadedRef` is a ref, not state, so the timer's stale-closure check (`if (!loadedRef.current) setIframeError(true)`) reads the live value. ThisWeekCard uses a state version (`loaded`) with `// eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` — both patterns work; using a ref here avoids the eslint suppression.
- The fallback wrapper uses `h-[400px]` to match the iframe's fixed height, so the card doesn't jump in size when the error state engages.

### Draft thank you on Campaigns + Weekly Gifts (2026-05-25)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00508-nvz`. Extends the ThankYouModal surface from Guardian Circle only to two more places — every campaign accordion in the Campaigns tab, and every donor side panel in Weekly Gifts. Both follow the GuardianCirclePage wiring pattern; ThankYouModal / ConstituentTable / draft-thank-you route all untouched.

- **`CampaignsTab.tsx`**: imports `ThankYouModal`; adds `thankYouTarget: { constituent: Constituent; campaignName: string } | null` state. The inner `<ConstituentTable compact …>` now gets `onThankYouClick={(c) => setThankYouTarget({ constituent: c, campaignName: displayName })}`. `displayName` is the fund-card label (the `OP:`-stripped fund name), which is the only place that knows which campaign the donor row belongs to — capturing it at click time avoids threading a third dimension into the Constituent type. Modal mounted at the bottom of the return, gated on `thankYouTarget`. Result: every fund's accordion expansion now shows the "Draft thank you" button next to each donor row, and clicking it opens a modal pre-loaded with that campaign's name.
- **`WeeklyGiftsTab.tsx`**: imports `ThankYouModal` + `type Constituent` from `ConstituentTable`. Adds the same shape `thankYouTarget` state. **Did NOT widen the existing `donorPanel` state** — the side panel already lazy-loads `donorData` (donor history) when it opens, and that response carries the most recent gift (`donorData.giftsByYear[0].gifts[0]`) which is what we thank the donor for. Synthesizes a `Constituent` on click: `donorId = String(donorPanel.id)`, `donorName = donorPanel.name`, `paid = donorData.totalGiving`, `lastGiftAmount` + `lastGiftDate` from the latest gift, `giftType = latest.isPledgePayment ? 'pledge' : 'donation'`, `totalPledge` / `outstanding` default to `0` (Weekly Gifts doesn't track pledge aggregates per donor). `campaignName = latest.event || latest.fund || 'SAR Academy'` — the gift's own event/fund label feeds the prompt's `isEventGift` heuristic.
- **Button placement (Weekly Gifts)**: full-width blue button at the top of the donor side panel scroll area, above the existing Donor notes + tags block. Disabled until `donorData` loads (the lastGift fields are required for a sensible prompt). Tooltip swaps to "Loading donor history…" while disabled.
- **No new fetches**: the modal call path remains `apiFetch('/api/development/draft-thank-you')` from inside `ThankYouModal`. The donor history fetch that powers the button was already happening when the side panel opens — no extra network on the new path.

**Gotchas**:
- Weekly Gifts "Draft thank you" thanks for the **most recent gift in `donorData`** (= the donor's latest gift in the cache), not necessarily the row the user clicked to open the panel. In practice these are usually the same gift since panels are opened from gift rows, but if a donor has multiple gifts and the user opened the panel from an older one, the modal will still target the most recent. Acceptable today because the panel is donor-focused (it shows a giving history), not gift-focused.
- Campaigns: the fund label uses `displayName` (with `OP:` stripped). That string flows directly into the Anthropic system prompt's `isEventGift` heuristic at `route.ts:buildUserMessage` — campaigns whose `displayName` contains `'guardian circle'` / `'scholarship'` / `'annual'` are classified as "general" (opening pattern (a)); everything else gets event-style opening (b). Pre-existing pattern; nothing to tune for these wirings.
- The `donorPanel` state on Weekly Gifts is *not* extended to carry the originally-clicked gift. If a future iteration wants per-clicked-gift thank-yous (e.g. for a refund correction), seed gift info onto the state at the click sites at lines ~449 + ~512.
- Both files mount `<ThankYouModal>` as a sibling of the regular content. Since the modal manages its own backdrop (`fixed inset-0`), it overlays whatever's behind it correctly without z-index conflicts.

### Thank-you draft prompt rewrite — voice-aligned with Rabbi Krauss's actual letters (2026-05-25)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00506-44x`. Tunes the AI draft output to match the principal's real voice and tone.

- **`app/api/development/draft-thank-you/route.ts`**: `SYSTEM_PROMPT` replaced wholesale. The old 1-sentence "3-4 sentences, sincere, no 'generous', sign 'Rabbi Krauss'" prompt is gone. The new prompt includes explicit voice and style rules (first-name greetings, 2–3 short paragraphs, opening-pattern (a) for general gifts and (b) for event sponsorships, Jewish-language guidance, sign-off variants) plus **4 real letters Rabbi Krauss has actually written** as exemplars (Shavuot appeal / Sephardic siddurim program / Siddur Play event / scholarship gift with student name). The system prompt is still cached via `cache_control: ephemeral` so the much larger token count doesn't repeat costs across calls in the same 5-minute window.
- **`buildUserMessage(req)` rewritten**: more structured input + an explicit `isEventGift` heuristic. A `campaignName` whose lowercased form omits "guardian circle" / "scholarship" / "annual" is treated as an event/program gift and the user message instructs the model to use opening pattern (b); otherwise pattern (a). The user message also threads through `gift date`, total pledge + outstanding amounts (when applicable). Currency formatting now lives inline (Intl.NumberFormat) rather than via the route's `formatMoney` helper — that helper stays in place for the `fallbackDraft` template path.
- **`max_tokens: 400` → `700`**: the new prompts produce longer, more thoughtful letters (the examples themselves are 100–180 tokens each; the model needs headroom to draft a multi-paragraph note + sign-off without truncation).
- **No client changes**: `ThankYouModal.tsx`, `ConstituentTable.tsx`, `GuardianCirclePage.tsx`, and the rest of the development surface area are untouched. The Gmail draft + Veracross BCC + signature attachment behavior is unchanged.

**Gotchas**:
- The `isEventGift` heuristic is brittle if SAR ever names a campaign with the word "guardian circle" embedded in an event title (e.g. "Guardian Circle Spring Reception"). If that happens, the heuristic will mis-classify it as a general gift and use opening pattern (a). Switch to an explicit `giftCategory` field on the request payload then.
- The system prompt is now ~1.5KB of static text (real example letters). With `cache_control: ephemeral` the first call in a 5-minute window pays the full prompt-token cost; subsequent calls hit the cache. If you see anomalous Anthropic spend, check that the cache header is still being honored.
- The new prompt does NOT mention `donorEmail`, `event` (Veracross gift event field), or `fund` (e.g. "OP: Cooper Fund"). When the modal sends those, only `campaignName` (which the modal hardcodes to "Guardian Circle 2025-26") reaches the model. The richer event/fund context the route's TypeScript signature accepts (`donorEmail`) is sent via the modal but unused by the prompt builder — wire it in if a future iteration wants per-fund tone.

### Attendance Distribution — Year to Date charts on the Absences page (2026-05-25)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00504-dmq`. New section appended below the existing today-attendance block on the Absences page with two server-aggregated charts.

- **API extension** (`app/api/absences/route.ts`): the existing GET now branches on `?view=ytd`. The default path (no param) is unchanged — same response shape, same Veracross calls, no perf delta. The `ytd` branch skips Veracross entirely and aggregates from `attendance_cache`:
  - Single paginated SELECT: `person_id, attendance_date, attendance_category, grade_level_id` where `attendance_category IN (1, 2)` (absent + tardy), `grade_level_id IN (allowedGrades)`, `attendance_date >= schoolYearStart`. Bypasses Supabase's 1000-row limit via `.range()` loop, matching the existing `monthlyTrend` / `topAbsentees` pagination pattern.
  - In one pass over the rows, builds: `absentByPerson` map (counts cat=1 per student), `personGrade` map (last-seen grade per student), and `quarterEvents` accumulator (per Q1–Q4, separately tracking `absences` + `tardies`).
  - `totalSchoolDays` = distinct `attendance_date` values across the in-scope rows. Fallback to 1 to avoid div-by-zero before any sync runs.
  - Tier computation: for each student in the absent map, `ada = (totalSchoolDays - absent) / totalSchoolDays`; bucketed `<0.9` chronically absent, `0.9–0.95` at risk, `≥0.95` satisfactory. Grouped by `grade_level_id`, sorted via new `GRADE_DISPLAY_ORDER` constant `[40, 35, …, 1–12]` so Pre-K → 12 renders in human order.
  - Returns `{ view: 'ytd', schoolYearStart, currentQuarter, totalSchoolDays, absenceTiersByGrade, quarterlyTrend }`. Error path also returns 200 with empty arrays so the client never breaks the page.
- **Helper functions** added to the same route:
  - `schoolYearStartIso(today)` — picks Sep 1 of the current or prior calendar year depending on whether we're past Sep.
  - `quarterForDate(dateIso, startYear)` — returns `'Q1' | 'Q2' | 'Q3' | 'Q4' | null` using lexicographic ISO string comparison (works because YYYY-MM-DD sorts chronologically). Date ranges match the spec exactly: Q1 Sep 1 – Nov 15, Q2 Nov 16 – Jan 31, Q3 Feb 1 – Apr 15, Q4 Apr 16 – Jun 30.
- **GRADE_LABELS extended** with `9–12` (was Pre-K through 8th only) so HS students render with their grade name in the tier chart. `GET(request)` now takes a `NextRequest` arg for the `?view=` query param read.
- **Dashboard.tsx changes**:
  - New `AbsencesYtdData` interface mirrors the route response.
  - New state: `absencesYtdData`, `absencesYtdLoading`, `loadedAbsencesYtdRef` (one-shot gate so panel re-opens don't re-fire the query).
  - New `useEffect` keyed on `activeNav === 'absences'` fires the lazy fetch once per session. Uses `apiFetch` so impersonation headers ride along — the YTD aggregation respects the impersonated user's divisions.
  - Section appended directly above the closing absences `</div>` (line ~7704):
    - Header with bar-chart icon + "Attendance Distribution — Year to Date" title.
    - Two-column grid (`grid grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-2 gap-6`) collapsing to one column on mobile.
    - **Chart 1** Recharts BarChart `layout="vertical"`, stacked: `chronically_absent` (#f87171) + `at_risk` (#fb923c) + `satisfactory` (#34d399). Y-axis grade labels, dynamic height `max(rows × 34 + 30, 200)`. Custom legend row above (Recharts default legend is too verbose for these short keys).
    - **Chart 2** Recharts BarChart vertical, grouped: `absences` (#f87171) + `tardies` (#fb923c) by quarter. Subtitle appends "· Q[N] in progress" when `currentQuarter` is non-null.
    - `ShimmerCards count={2}` while loading; "No year-to-date attendance data available yet." empty state.
- **Division filter parity**: both the route and the client lazy-fetch use the existing `getEffectiveDivisions(session)` + `getGradeFilterForMember()` plumbing, so impersonation + `?division=` toggle behavior is identical to the live today-attendance block.
- **Gotchas / notes**:
  - The cache only stores non-present records, so the tier chart's denominator is "distinct dates with at least one event," not "all calendar school days." In practice at SAR every school day has at least one absence, so the numbers match. If a future year has perfect attendance days (unlikely), tier % would be slightly inflated.
  - Tier chart only counts students who had ≥1 absent or tardy row this year. Perfect-attendance students don't appear at all — they'd all stack into "Satisfactory," so the chart's story is unchanged, but a hypothetical "0 students total" grade would render as a blank row. Acceptable approximation; pulling the full Veracross roster would double the request latency.
  - The new helper functions (`schoolYearStartIso`, `quarterForDate`, `GRADE_DISPLAY_ORDER`) are scoped to this route file. If admissions or another module needs them, lift to `lib/schoolYear.ts`.
  - Aggregation is single-request on full attendance_cache scan (~90k rows). Tested via build — no measurable hit. If it gets slow at >250k rows, push the GROUP BY into a Postgres RPC.

### Four-bug fix pass: task source/prefix, admissions tag palette, expandable task cards, author display name (2026-05-24)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00502-wbf`. Four independent fixes that landed in one deploy.

- **BUG 1 — Task source/prefix routes by constituent_name (`donor-notes/route.ts`)**: previously every auto-created task hard-coded `source: 'development'` and title prefix `[Development]` regardless of which context generated the note. Notes on admissions students (`"Admissions: <name>"`) now produce `source: 'admissions'` + `[Admissions]` prefix, so they surface in the **From Admissions** section of the Tasks view instead of From Development. Cooper/Israel-tagged notes get `[Cooper Fund]` / `[Israel Fund]` prefixes but stay on `source: 'development'` (those are still funded by Development). New helper `getSourceFromConstituent(constituentName)` returns `{ source, label }` from the prefix; falls back to `{ source: 'development', label: 'Development' }` for any unprefixed donor name (Guardian Circle, Weekly Gifts, etc.). The Slack DM `sendTaskSlack` call also uses the resolved `source` so cross-channel routing stays consistent.
- **BUG 2 — Admissions tag palette (`DonorAnnotations.tsx` + admissions drilldown + `donor-tags/route.ts`)**:
  - **New exported `TagDef` type** with `{ label, color, bg? }`. `bg` is optional — when present, the pill renders via Tailwind classes (soft tint); when absent, the pill renders via inline-style hex (saturated, legacy development behavior).
  - **`DonorAnnotations` accepts optional `tagDefs?: TagDef[]` prop** which threads to `TagPills`. Defaults to module-level `TAG_DEFS` (the 5 development tags). `handleApplyTag` validates against the resolved list, not the module-level constant — so admissions-only labels pass the client-side gate too.
  - **`TagPills` updated**: applied-pill renderer looks up the pill's label in the resolved palette via `Map<label, TagDef>`. If the def has `bg`, renders `<button className="${bg} ${color}">`; else falls back to `style={{ backgroundColor: t.color }}` with `text-white` (existing development look). The picker dropdown's swatch follows the same pattern — Tailwind class when `bg` is set, inline hex when not.
  - **Admissions palette** is a new module-level `ADMISSIONS_TAG_DEFS` constant in `Dashboard.tsx`: 5 labels with `text-…-700` color + `bg-…-50` (or `bg-slate-100`) background. Passed to the `<DonorAnnotations>` inside the admissions student drilldown only — Guardian Circle, Cooper, Israel, and Weekly Gifts call sites stay unchanged.
  - **Server-side allowlist (`donor-tags/route.ts`)** extended with all 5 admissions labels + chosen hex colors (`#f59e0b` / `#ef4444` / `#8b5cf6` / `#3b82f6` / `#64748b`). The stored hex doesn't affect admissions pill rendering — the client uses its own Tailwind classes — but keeps the historical `donor_tags.color` column populated with a reasonable value in case a future consumer reads it directly. **Label collision**: "Needs Follow-Up" (dev) and "Needs Follow-up" (admissions) differ only by case on the second word. Both map to `#f59e0b`. They're treated as distinct strings by the allowlist (`Record<string, string>` is case-sensitive), so dev and admissions versions are separate rows in `donor_tags`. If a context ever needs unified lookup, normalize case at the boundary.
- **BUG 3 — Clickable + expandable task cards (`Dashboard.tsx`)**: From Development + From Admissions cards used to render a flat title + source_ref with no way to see the task's description. Now each pending card is clickable to toggle expansion. New state `expandedSourcedTaskId: string | null` (one open at a time across both sections). Cards get `cursor-pointer + hover:bg-slate-50` and a chevron in the right of the header that rotates 180° when open. Expanded panel (below the header, separated by `border-t`) renders:
  - `description` (if non-null) in `text-sm text-slate-600 whitespace-pre-wrap max-h-40 overflow-y-auto`.
  - A "Mark complete" button styled emerald/amber to match the section's accent color, which calls the existing `toggleSourcedTaskStatus(t)` (which already PATCHes `/api/tasks` with `status: 'done'`).
  - `e.stopPropagation()` on the expanded panel + on the checkbox button so clicks inside don't re-collapse the card.
  - The `SourcedTask` interface gained a `description: string | null` field. The `/api/tasks` GET already SELECTed `description`, so no API change needed.
- **BUG 4 — Author display name (`donor-notes/route.ts` GET + `DonorAnnotations.tsx`)**: notes used to render the raw `created_by` email (e.g. `kraussb@saracademy.org`). The GET handler now collects all unique `created_by` emails from the response, batch-fetches `display_name` from `workspace_members`, and adds `author_name` to each note (falls back to `created_by` if no match). The `DonorNote` interface gained `author_name?: string | null`. The notes list renders `{n.author_name || n.created_by}` so the display gracefully falls back during the rollout / for emails that have no `workspace_members` row.

**Out of scope per spec**: Tag behavior on development pages (Guardian Circle, Cooper, Israel) — they continue to use the module-level `TAG_DEFS` since they don't pass `tagDefs`. Slack DM logic and Google Tasks integration both unchanged (the Slack DM call now uses the resolved `source` string but the message format is the same). No other files modified.

### Google Tasks integration (2026-05-24)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00499-26p`. Per-member Google Tasks OAuth + automatic Tasks push when a connected member is @mentioned in a donor note.

**Required env var on Cloud Run** (set manually before next user verification — the deploy works without it but the Sign-in flow won't complete until set):
- `GOOGLE_TASKS_REDIRECT_URI=https://rbk-cmd-center.web.app/api/google-tasks-callback` — both the auth and callback routes fall back to `${NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL}/api/google-tasks-callback` if this env var is missing, so the default path also works as long as NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL is set (it is).
- **Google Cloud Console** — the same redirect URI must be added to the OAuth client's Authorized redirect URIs list, alongside the existing Gmail callback URI. Without this, the OAuth flow returns `redirect_uri_mismatch`.
- `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` and `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` — reused from the existing Gmail OAuth flow. No new secret required.

**New DB column**: `workspace_members.google_tasks_refresh_token text` (migration `workspace_members_google_tasks_refresh_token` applied via Supabase MCP). Null = not connected. Per-member, so each user connects independently.

**New routes**:
- `GET /api/google-tasks-auth/route.ts` — auth-gated initiation. Builds the Google OAuth consent URL with scope `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/tasks`, `access_type=offline`, `prompt=consent` (the `prompt=consent` is required to re-issue a refresh_token on re-authorization). `state = session.user.email` for callback lookup. Redirects to Google.
- `GET /api/google-tasks-callback/route.ts` — exchanges `?code=` for tokens, looks up the `workspace_members` row by `state` (case-insensitive email), writes `refresh_token` to `google_tasks_refresh_token`. Redirects to `/home?tasks_connected=1` on success or `/home?tasks_error=1&reason=…` on any failure (`missing_code_or_state`, `token_exchange_failed`, `no_refresh_token_revoke_and_retry`, `member_not_found`, `db_write_failed`).

**New lib helper** `lib/googleTasks.ts`:
- `getAccessTokenFromRefresh(refreshToken)` — POSTs to `oauth2.googleapis.com/token` with `grant_type=refresh_token`, returns the short-lived access token or `null` on failure.
- `createGoogleTaskForMember({ refreshToken, title, notes?, dueIso? })` — POSTs to `tasks.googleapis.com/tasks/v1/lists/@default/tasks`. Default `due` is tomorrow at midnight UTC (so the item lands in the user's "due tomorrow" bucket). Returns `boolean`; never throws.

**Session payload extended** (`app/api/auth/session/route.ts` POST + `app/api/auth/session/workspace/route.ts` GET): both now return `google_tasks_connected: boolean`, computed as `!!memberRow.google_tasks_refresh_token` for the active member. The SELECT in the session POST handler was widened to include `google_tasks_refresh_token`.

**`AuthProvider` / `useWorkspace()`**: `WorkspaceContextType` gained `googleTasksConnected: boolean`. Set on initial state, the signed-in load path, and both signed-out reset branches. Impersonation deliberately uses the *real* session's flag, not the impersonated user's — the Sidebar UI in impersonation mode is still the impersonator's Tasks state.

**Sidebar UI**: new optional `googleTasksConnected?: boolean` prop. Renders a small row above the user-avatar block (separated by a thin slate divider, no other layout change). When `false`: "Connect Google Tasks" link → `/api/google-tasks-auth`. When `true`: muted emerald "✓ Google Tasks connected" line. Shown for all authenticated users (not module-gated). `Dashboard.tsx` + `app/home/page.tsx` both destructure `googleTasksConnected` from `useWorkspace()` and pass it through. `app/admin/permissions/page.tsx` does not pass it — the prop default of `false` keeps the admin page rendering as before.

**Donor-notes integration** (`app/api/development/donor-notes/route.ts`): after the Command Center `tasks` row is inserted for a mentioned user, the route bulk-fetches `google_tasks_refresh_token` for all mentioned members in one query (`SELECT id, google_tasks_refresh_token WHERE id IN (...)`) and pushes a matching item to each connected user's Google Tasks list via `createGoogleTaskForMember`. The push is fire-and-forget (`void`-wrapped) — failure logs but never blocks the note insert, the Slack mention DM, or the assignment Slack DM. Title matches the CC task exactly; notes field is `"From RBK Command Center — donor note on <constituentName>"`.

**Gotchas**:
- `state` carries only the user's email, not workspace_id. The callback's `workspace_members` lookup uses `email ILIKE` and `.limit(1)` — fine for the current SAR setup where a member exists in exactly one workspace, but if a future user joins multiple workspaces the wrong row could be updated. Extend `state` to include workspace_id (base64 JSON, mirroring the gmail-consent pattern) before that scenario lands.
- `prompt=consent` forces re-consent every time. If users find that noisy on re-auth, switch to `prompt=select_account` and handle the no-refresh-token edge case by surfacing the manual revoke link.
- The lib helper logs warnings on token-exchange failure but does not auto-clear the stale `google_tasks_refresh_token` from the DB. If Google revokes the grant (e.g. user revoked at myaccount.google.com/permissions), the Sidebar will keep showing "connected" but pushes will silently fail. Add a cleanup path (delete the column on `invalid_grant`) if this surfaces.
- Impersonation: `googleTasksConnected` reflects the impersonator's real session state, not the impersonated user's. Becca impersonating RBK will see "✓ Google Tasks connected" if Becca herself is connected, even if RBK isn't. Intentional — the Sidebar surfaces the Sign-out affordance for the real session, and the Tasks state belongs alongside that.
- The admin page (`app/admin/permissions/page.tsx`) renders Sidebar without the new prop — the default of `false` makes it look "not connected" there even for users who actually are. Low-stakes since admin page is Becca-only; thread the prop through if it matters.

### Revert donor-notes owner-role task gate (2026-05-24)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00497-8hs`. The prior deploy (`ssrrbkcmdcenter-00495-l97`) restricted auto-task creation in `/api/development/donor-notes` to mentions of owner-role members only — but that broke the workflow for Emily, Sara, and Leora, who need formal task rows when @mentioned, not just the Slack DM. Reverted.

- **New rule** (replaces the owner-role gate): every @mentioned user with a non-null `assignee_key` gets a task row. The targeted `SELECT id, role` query and `taskTargets` filter were both removed — `mentioned.filter(u => u.assignee_key)` is the only gate now.
- **Slack DM behavior unchanged** — mention DMs still fire for every mentioned user with a `slack_user_id`, regardless of role or assignee_key presence.
- **Members without an `assignee_key`** (rare; only happens if a new workspace member hasn't been onboarded with a Tasks-column key) get the mention DM but no task row, since `tasks.assigned_to` requires a recognized Capitalized key.
- **Net effect**: behavior identical to the `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00493-cc6` deploy and earlier — the owner-role experiment was a brief detour that landed in two revisions.

### Mentionable users now dynamic from workspace_members (2026-05-24)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00495-l97`. The hardcoded `MENTIONABLE_USERS` list in `DonorAnnotations.tsx` (5 SAR staff) was blocking newly-added members from being @-mentionable without a code change. Now driven from `workspace_members`.

- **New route `app/api/workspace/mentionable-users/route.ts`** — auth-gated; reads `workspace_id` via `getEffectiveWorkspaceId(session)` (impersonation-aware). Optional `?module=<key>` filter: when present, returns members where `role = 'owner'` OR `allowed_modules->{module}.enabled = true` (handles both legacy `true` and granular `{enabled, ...}` shapes). When absent, returns every workspace member. Returns `{ users: [{ id, name, fullName, email, slackId }] }` sorted by `display_name ASC`. `name` derivation: `assignee_key || display_name.split(' ')[0] || email-local-part` — matches the @-name semantics the parser expects.
- **`DonorAnnotations.tsx` updates**:
  - Exported `MentionableUser` interface.
  - Hardcoded `MENTIONABLE_USERS` constant **kept** as both the initial seed state (so the @ dropdown is never empty during the round-trip) and a defensive fallback if the fetch fails.
  - `MentionText` accepts an optional `users` prop (defaults to `MENTIONABLE_USERS`). Mention regex is now built per-render via `useMemo(() => buildMentionPattern(users), [users])` so newly-added workspace members get pill rendering, not just autocomplete. Names are regex-escaped defensively (current `assignee_key`s are alphanumeric but future ones may not be).
  - `NotesList` accepts an optional `users` prop, threads it to both the candidate filter (autocomplete dropdown) and the `<MentionText>` it renders inside the notes list.
  - `DonorAnnotations` default export accepts new optional `mentionableUsers?: MentionableUser[]` prop. If provided, used directly. If absent, fetches `GET /api/workspace/mentionable-users` on mount (with `MENTIONABLE_USERS` seeded as initial state). The fetch is gated by `mentionableUsers` being undefined — caller-provided list short-circuits the network call.
  - The resolved list (`mentionableUsers ?? fetchedUsers`) is passed to `NotesList` so every consumer — autocomplete dropdown, MentionText pills inside saved notes — uses the same source of truth.
- **`/api/development/donor-notes/route.ts` updates**:
  - Server-side mention parsing was already dynamic (using `getWorkspaceMembers()` from `lib/getWorkspaceMemberByAssignee.ts`) — no change needed there.
  - **Task auto-creation restricted** to owner-role mentions. Previously every @mention created a task; now only mentions where `role = 'owner'` (or defensively `assignee_key = 'RBK'`) get a `tasks` row. Non-owner mentions still get the Slack mention DM — they just don't generate a formal task. Roles fetched via a targeted `SELECT id, role WHERE id IN (mentionedIds)` query rather than expanding `WorkspaceMemberLookup`, so the lib type stays unchanged for now.
  - Dedup is implicit: `parseMentions` already returns a unique list of members.
- **Out of scope / not changed**:
  - `lib/getWorkspaceMemberByAssignee.ts` — `WorkspaceMemberLookup` does not yet include `role`. Donor-notes does an inline query instead. Extend the helper when a second caller needs role.
  - All call sites of `DonorAnnotations` (`GuardianCirclePage`, `CooperFundTab`, `IsraelFundTab`, the admissions drilldown in `Dashboard.tsx`) — the new prop is optional, so they continue to work unchanged. They benefit from the dynamic fetch automatically.
  - The `Slack DM message format`, `tag picking behavior`, `note save/delete`, and the standalone `MentionText` rendering logic outside the regex source — all unchanged per spec.
- **Behavioral notes**:
  - **Workflow regression for non-owner mentions**: Emily / Sara / Leora used to get auto-task rows when @mentioned. Now they only get the Slack DM. If that breaks Emily's workflow, revert by lifting the `taskTargets` filter or by adding `assignee_key IN ('Emily')` to the OR clause. Becca's role is `owner` so @Becca mentions still create tasks.
  - **No module filter applied** at call sites — per the spec, the simpler "if you're in the workspace you're mentionable" model. Module filtering is supported by the route (`?module=`) for future use but no caller passes it.

### DonorAnnotations on the Admissions enrollment drilldown (2026-05-24)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00493-cc6`. Brings per-student notes + tags to the Admissions side panel so admissions staff can attach context to individual applicants/re-enrollees using the same `donor_notes` / `donor_tags` tables that Guardian Circle, Cooper, and Israel already use.

- **Where it renders**: the projection-combined drilldown panel (the one that opens when a staff user clicks a By Grade row in the Enrollment Projection table). Each student card in that panel is now click-to-expand — clicking toggles a chevron + reveals a `Notes & Tags` block below the existing student details (status badges + grade-override + Pisgah toggle).
- **Namespacing**: `constituentName = "Admissions: " + r.name`, `constituentId = "admissions-" + String(r.personId)`. The prefix keeps these rows out of donor-level annotations. `r.name` and `r.personId` are the existing field names on the drilldown's `combinedRows` projection — confirmed in place, not guessed.
- **State plumbing (`Dashboard.tsx`)**:
  - `admissionsDrilldownTags: Map<string, DonorTag[]>` — workspace-wide tag bulk fetch keyed on full prefixed constituent_name.
  - `loadedAdmissionsTagsRef = useRef(false)` — gates the fetch to fire once per session, on the **first** drilldown open. Re-opening the panel does not re-fire the query.
  - `expandedAdmissionsStudent: string | null` — which card is open (one at a time). Keyed as `${r.source}-${r.personId}`. Reset to `null` whenever `admissionsDrilldown` changes (i.e. user clicks into a different grade).
  - `updateAdmissionsTag(constituentName, newTags)` — `useCallback`-wrapped; updates the map in place via `new Map(prev)` → set/delete (mirrors `GuardianCirclePage.handleTagsChange`).
- **Bulk fetch**: lazy `useEffect` keyed on `admissionsDrilldown` that checks `loadedAdmissionsTagsRef.current` before firing. Calls `GET /api/development/donor-tags` (no `constituent_name` filter), groups by `constituent_name`, sets the map. The same query returns every donor tag in the workspace — only admissions-prefixed keys are looked up at render time, so unrelated entries are silently ignored.
- **Dynamic import**: `const DonorAnnotations = dynamic(() => import('./development/DonorAnnotations'), { ssr: false });` — matches the pattern in `ConstituentTable.tsx`, `WeeklyGiftsTab.tsx`, `CooperFundTab.tsx`, `IsraelFundTab.tsx`. The `DonorTag` type comes in via `import type { DonorTag } from './development/DonorAnnotations'` at the top with the other static imports.
- **Click-handling**: card-level `onClick` toggles `expandedAdmissionsStudent`. `e.stopPropagation()` added to the existing grade-override / Pisgah-toggle row container and to the new annotations expansion block so those interactive children don't bubble up and re-toggle the card. The Veracross "open in" anchor already had its own `stopPropagation`.
- **Out of scope (per spec)**: `DonorAnnotations.tsx`, the donor-notes / donor-tags API routes, Guardian Circle, and all other files untouched. Only `Dashboard.tsx` changed.
- **Gotchas**: (a) only the **projection_combined** drilldown got the annotations panel — other drilldown variants (`projection_pending`, `projection_pisgah`, `projection_incomplete`, `projection_waitlist`, `projection_leaving`, plus the non-projection `grade`/`status`/`response` types) still render their own student-card layouts without annotations. If the user wants annotations on those too, the same pattern can be cloned per panel. (b) The `loadedAdmissionsTagsRef` is a session-lifetime cache — if another tab writes a new tag, it won't reflect here until a hard refresh. Acceptable for the current single-user workflow; switch to a re-fetch on panel open if multi-user concurrency becomes an issue. (c) The annotations panel inherits the click-anywhere-on-card UX from Cooper/Israel — clicking the card body toggles. If the user has unsaved text in the notes input, the next outside-card click could collapse the panel; the existing `DonorAnnotations` notes input submits on Enter or button-click, so transient text won't be lost as long as the user finalizes before clicking away.

### Development mobile audit: Cooper bar charts wrapped in overflow-x-auto (2026-05-24)

Cloud Run revision `ssrrbkcmdcenter-00491-d26`. Mobile-pass audit of all 5 Development tab components against the project's mobile standards (stat grids `grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-X`, tables wrapped in `overflow-x-auto` with `min-w`, side-by-side layouts using `md:` breakpoint).

- **`CooperFundTab.tsx`** — Both Recharts horizontal `BarChart` blocks (Disbursements by Category + Money In · By Event) were rendering inside a plain `<ResponsiveContainer width="100%">` with no horizontal scroll fallback. At 375px the YAxis category labels (140px and 180px reserved respectively) ate so much of the viewport that the bar area collapsed to a sliver.
